# ProtoPulse Master Backlog

> **Single source of truth** for tracked ProtoPulse work: bugs, security fixes, features, tech debt, UX polish, parity gaps, and moonshots.

> This file intentionally preserves both open and completed items so priority, provenance, and completion history stay searchable in one place.

## How to Use This Document

- **Pick from the top.** Items are grouped by priority (P0 → P3) then by domain.
- **IDs are stable.** Each item has a `BL-XXXX` ID. Reference these in commits, PRs, wave notes, and follow-up docs.
- **Cross-refs** show the original ID from the source doc (MF-xxx, UX-xxx, IFX-xxx, ARDX-xxx, etc.) for traceability.
- **Open and done work both stay here.** Do not remove items just because they are completed; update status instead so historical context remains searchable.
- **When you finish an item**, change its status to `DONE` and add the Wave plus commit/PR reference when available.

## Backlog Conventions

- **`OPEN`** = not started.
- **`PARTIAL`** = some implementation exists, but the user-visible workflow or acceptance criteria are not complete.
- **`BLOCKED`** = waiting on a prerequisite, dependency, or external decision.
- **`DONE`** = implemented. Add the Wave number and any verification detail available.
- **`DONE (verified Wave N)`** = implemented earlier and explicitly re-checked in that wave.
- **Preservation rule:** unless Tyler explicitly approves pruning or archival, keep every tracked item in this document and prefer notes/cross-links over deletion.
- **Update discipline:** when Quick Stats or the latest Wave changes, update both the top snapshot and the footer note in the same edit so they do not drift.

## Change Log

- **2026-06-27:** `/project-reflection` (2nd run, diffing the 2026-06-24 baseline) opened **`BL-0902`** (renumbered **`BL-1093`** 2026-07-10 — see below, ID collided with the unrelated AI-eval-harness BL-0902) — refactor high-cyclomatic-complexity `server/` functions (`ingressPart` CCN 109, `getStandardLayers` CCN 75 / 362 NLOC, the `server/export/*` family). First new backlog item since Wave 153's "fully DONE" snapshot. Reflection also closed the standing P0 AI-effectiveness gap (engine-verification/`earlyoom` note added to `AGENTS.md`, commit `f74b29d5`) and untracked `ds-bundle/` generated artifacts. Engine `packages/` remains complexity-clean (211k→284k LOC, emu campaign). P2 open count: 0 → 1. See memory `project_reflection_2026_06_27`.
- **2026-04-18:** Wave 153 — closed `BL-0126` (Shared unit/scale contract across sim + DRC engines). Verified Phase 2 wiring had already landed across the codebase: `shared/drc-engine.ts` imports `Length_mil` from `./units` (5 references); `client/src/lib/board-stackup.ts` consumes branded `Length_mil` from `@shared/units`; sim↔DRC boundary `client/src/lib/simulation/pcb-geometry-bridge.ts` imports `milToMm`/`mmToMeter` from `@shared/units`; simulation suite (`spice-netlist-parser`, `spice-generator`, `design-var-spice-bridge`, `thermal-analysis`) all import canonical helpers from `@shared/units`. Both engines structurally consume the single canonical module — parity is implicit. No new code required — the backlog note was stale; Phase 2 lived in the codebase already. P2 open count: 1 → 0. Backlog fully DONE (508/508).
- **2026-04-17:** Wave 152 — closed `BL-0524` (Conflict resolution UI). BL-0486 (CRDT conflict resolution algorithm) had landed in Wave 67 but the losing-op UX was never surfaced — server silently dropped LWW-losers, insert-superseded, and delete-rejected operations. Added `detectConflict()` + `Conflict` + `ConflictKind` types in `shared/collaboration.ts` (9 new tests), instrumented `server/collaboration.ts#mergeAndBroadcastOps` to emit a server→loser `conflict-detected` WS message with the losing op + the authoritative winning op (no dedicated REST resolve route — "accept mine" re-uses the existing `state-update` path with a fresh Lamport timestamp). Extended `CollaborationClient` with `pendingConflicts`/`resolveConflict`/`conflicts-change` event + `dismissConflict` (4 new client tests). New `client/src/components/collaboration/ConflictResolutionDialog.tsx` shadcn Dialog with side-by-side yours/theirs panels, editable merge textarea, per-conflict paging, and Accept-mine / Accept-theirs / Save-custom-merge buttons (5 UI tests). Wired into `ShareProjectButton` as an amber conflict-count badge with `Alt+K` shortcut and auto-open on first conflict. Lock enforcement from BL-0487 untouched (17 integration tests still green, 23 CRDT + 44 auth + 50 collab server tests pass). P2 open count: 2 → 1; BLOCKED count: 1 → 0.
- **2026-04-17:** Wave 151 — closed `BL-0150` (Inventory tracking tied to BOM consumption). Added pure `computeShortfall()` + `BomShortfall` type in `shared/parts/shortfall.ts` (canonical rule: `max(0, quantityNeeded - coalesce(quantityOnHand, 0))` — supply is never decremented on BOM writes to prevent double-booking). New `BomStorage.getShortfalls(projectId)` with SQL filter `needed > coalesce(onHand, 0)` and shortfall-DESC ordering. New route `GET /api/projects/:id/bom/shortfalls` returning `{ data, total, totalShortfallUnits }`. Client hook `useBomShortfalls` + `indexShortfallsByPartNumber` helper. Red "−N" shortfall badge (with `PackageX` icon) in `BomTable` stock cell, tooltip showing `BOM wants N / on hand M`. Export precheck `bomShortfallCheck` added to `fab-package`, `pick-place`, `bom-csv` runners as a non-blocking warning. Tests: 11 shared shortfall tests (boundary/null/NaN), 4 hook tests (indexing/query key), 3 endpoint shape tests, plus `getShortfalls` mock wired into `api.test.ts`. P2 open count: 3 → 2.
- **2026-04-14:** Wave 150 — closed `BL-0473` (MPN normalization and dedup in BOM). Added `shared/parts/mpn.ts` normalizer (trim, whitespace collapse, packaging-suffix strip for `/NOPB`, `#PBF`, `-TR*`, `-7`, `+`) with case-preserving + case-insensitive comparison helpers. Wired into `server/parts-ingress.ts findByMpn()` and `server/storage/parts.ts PartsStorage.getByMpn()` — case-insensitive `ilike` match plus manufacturer-scoped comparison-key fallback so `RC0402FR-0710KL`, `rc0402fr-0710kl`, and `LM317T/NOPB ≡ LM317T` all dedup. Stock upsert at existing `(projectId, partId)` already bumps quantity instead of duplicating — now reached reliably. Tests: 37 unit tests for the normalizer + 6 regression tests for ingress dedup (case variant, whitespace variant, stock-qty bump, suffix fallback, genuinely different MPN, empty-manufacturer skip). P2 open count: 4 → 3.
- **2026-04-14:** Refreshed `Next Up` — all 10 prior candidates were already DONE (verified Wave 106 / 107). Replaced the stale table with the 3 remaining `PARTIAL` P2 items (`BL-0126`, `BL-0150`, `BL-0473`); `BL-0524` remains `BLOCKED on BL-0486` and is excluded. Recounted Quick Stats directly from P0–P3 sections (508 rows total: 4 open, 504 done) and removed the outdated "BACKLOG COMPLETE" note.
- **2026-03-13:** Wave 80 — marked 5 P0 security items DONE (BL-0636/0637/0638/0639/0642). Updated blockers, decisions, ADR requirements, and discovery spikes as resolved by Pure-Local Desktop App pivot (ADR 0007/0008). Firmware/debugger/platform items now unblocked.
- **2026-03-13:** Added 15 missing backlog items from a repo-wide gap audit, covering authz/tenant-scoping gaps, RAG durability, async job execution, supplier realism, Kanban persistence, Circuit Code materialization, generative candidate adoption, and other cross-tool integration misses.
- **2026-03-13:** Added durable preplanning artifacts for the two biggest near-term C5 programs: firmware runtime/debugger and collaboration foundation/RBAC/branching.
- **2026-03-13:** Backfilled `C1`-`C5` complexity across all 493 tracked backlog rows, expanded the complexity radar to the top 25 open items, scored epics and wave candidates, and corrected manual snapshot drift at the top of the file.
- **2026-03-13:** Added a planning layer at the top of the backlog: health, recent wins, next-up queue, wave candidates, epics, discovery spikes, blocker tracking, decision points, planning fields, and item templates.
- **2026-03-13:** Clarified preservation rules, status semantics, and snapshot maintenance guidance; aligned the top snapshot and footer note with the Wave 76 state.

## Complexity Radar (Highest-Complexity Open Items)

Use this section to call out the largest open work by architectural scope and dependency load, not just calendar effort. This is the “if we start this, we are really starting a program of work” list. The top 25 below are the current first-pass highest-complexity open items.

| Rank | ID | Complexity | Work | Why It Is Hard |
|------|----|------------|------|----------------|
| 1 | `BL-0635` | `C5` | Arduino code simulation in the browser | Compile + execute + MCU simulation + serial + pin-state visualization + sensor input plumbing is effectively a new platform inside ProtoPulse. |
| 2 | `BL-0632` | `C5` | Hardware debugger integration | Crosses browser limits, local-agent/security design, debug-probe support, GDB workflows, and serious embedded UX expectations. |
| 3 | `BL-0631` | `C5` | Simulator-based firmware execution | Introduces a new runtime layer and is also a prerequisite decision for several downstream simulation features. |
| 4 | `BL-0381` | `C5` | RBAC + org/team tenancy | System-wide infrastructure touching auth, ownership, collaboration, auditability, and future team-facing features. |
| 5 | `BL-0571` | `C5` | Shared schematic ↔ breadboard netlist | Fritzing-class dual-view coherence requires a shared source of truth across two editors that currently do not talk to each other. |
| 6 | `BL-0370` | `C5` | Public API + webhook platform | Opens a durable external contract surface with auth, rate limits, event design, versioning, and long-term compatibility burden. |
| 7 | `BL-0371` | `C5` | Plugin / extension SDK | Requires safe extension points, capability boundaries, lifecycle hooks, packaging, and a public support surface. |
| 8 | `BL-0451` | `C5` | Full circuit + firmware + enclosure co-design | Spans EDA, firmware, 3D/mechanical, workflow orchestration, and verification across several product domains. |
| 9 | `BL-0454` | `C5` | Multi-board system orchestrator | Needs system-level design coordination across multiple boards, firmware surfaces, and likely deployment/runtime choreography. |
| 10 | `BL-0464` | `C5` | Time machine playback | Requires synchronized playback of firmware state, logs, schematic state, and likely timeline-aware restoration semantics. |
| 11 | `BL-0467` | `C5` | Mission mode | Bundles concept-to-kit workflow orchestration into a single guided product surface spanning many existing systems. |
| 12 | `BL-0461` | `C5` | Firmware-aware simulation mode | Bridges firmware execution with circuit simulation and educational visual feedback rather than keeping them separate. |
| 13 | `BL-0613` | `C5` | Multi-platform board support | Expands the workbench from Arduino-only assumptions into a broader embedded platform model with much larger support burden. |
| 14 | `BL-0614` | `C5` | Custom board definitions | Requires board-schema design, editor UX, build integration, memory/clock modeling, and a route from PCB data into firmware tooling. |
| 15 | `BL-0184` | `C5` | Design branching model | Branch semantics affect review, restore, audit history, merge tooling, and nearly every serious collaboration workflow. |
| 16 | `BL-0185` | `C5` | Merge tooling for branch diffs | Depends on the branching model and has to reconcile complex design-domain conflicts, not just text lines. |
| 17 | `BL-0376` | `C5` | Git-native design diff / merge | Would expose the internal design representation as a durable mergeable artifact with strong correctness demands. |
| 18 | `BL-0450` | `C5` | Idea-to-ordered-PCB in 30 minutes | This is a whole product-program goal that depends on strong automation across design, validation, BOM, manufacturing, and guidance. |
| 19 | `BL-0457` | `C5` | Self-healing assistant with approval gates | Touches AI autonomy, trust, safety, approval workflows, and rollback/verification pathways across the app. |
| 20 | `BL-0465` | `C5` | Design-to-drive mode | Requires translating hardware design into runnable test firmware and bench behaviors, not just generating code stubs. |
| 21 | `BL-0466` | `C5` | AI co-debugging wiring + firmware | Needs shared reasoning across two failure domains that currently have different data models and verification loops. |
| 22 | `BL-0630` | `C5` | Scratch-like visual Arduino programming | A whole additional programming surface with bidirectional block↔text sync and strong beginner UX expectations. |
| 23 | `BL-0633` | `C5` | ESP-IDF framework support | Adds a second serious firmware ecosystem with different project structure, tooling, and runtime assumptions. |
| 24 | `BL-0561` | `C4` | PDN/SI reads actual PCB geometry | Requires real geometry extraction and stable contracts between routed board data and advanced analysis engines. |
| 25 | `BL-0555` | `C4` | SI Advisor + PDN deep integration | Couples advanced analysis engines, shared geometry/state, and correctness-sensitive expert workflows. |

## Complex Work / Epics

Use these epic summaries when a single backlog row is no longer enough to plan or communicate the work. Parent epics should stay concise and point to the detailed child backlog IDs that carry the implementation load.

### Epic A — Simulation UX & Firmware-Aware Prototyping

- **Goal:** Turn simulation into a first-class, visually immediate workflow that makers can run without expert setup.
- **Why it matters:** This is one of the clearest paths to matching the “instant gratification” value people get from TinkerCAD and EveryCircuit.
- **Complexity:** `C5` — New runtime and cross-domain simulation/firmware decisions.
- **Scope included:** `BL-0121`, `BL-0128`, `BL-0514`, `BL-0560`, `BL-0573`, `BL-0574`, `BL-0619`, `BL-0620`, `BL-0622`, `BL-0461`, `BL-0635`.
- **Scope excluded for now:** `BL-0554` THD/IMD and other specialist analysis that can land after the core simulation UX is cohesive.
- **Dependencies:** Stable simulation control surface, result overlay strategy, and a decision on browser-only vs hybrid firmware execution.
- **Risks:** Solver/runtime cost, confusing UX if too many simulation modes remain visible at once, and keeping firmware/runtime state in sync with circuit state.
- **Open questions:** Should “Start Simulation” auto-detect mode always, or offer a fallback chooser when confidence is low? Does firmware execution require a local helper?
- **Exit criteria:** One obvious start flow, visual overlays on-canvas, component state rendering, and at least one supported firmware-aware simulation path.
- **Verification plan:** Run end-to-end sample circuits, compare numeric outputs against known fixtures, and verify visual state changes on canvas.
- **Related docs:** `docs/product-analysis-report.md`, `docs/product-analysis-checklist.md`, `docs/plans/2026-03-13-c5-firmware-runtime-program.md`.

### Epic B — Arduino Workbench / IDE Parity

- **Goal:** Make ProtoPulse’s Workbench credible as an everyday Arduino development environment, not just an export sidecar.
- **Why it matters:** The moment makers hit compile friction, serial confusion, or bad diagnostics, they leave for Arduino IDE or PlatformIO.
- **Complexity:** `C5` — Toolchain, editor, upload, diagnostics, and platform-support surface.
- **Scope included:** `BL-0140`-`BL-0157`, `BL-0515`-`BL-0518`, `BL-0584`, `BL-0598`-`BL-0618`, `BL-0400`-`BL-0426`.
- **Scope excluded for now:** Deep enterprise platform work that does not directly improve the maker edit→compile→upload→debug loop.
- **Dependencies:** Board/package model clarity, reliable CLI job orchestration, structured error parsing, and decisions on multi-platform support breadth.
- **Risks:** Toolchain sprawl, background job complexity, and trying to match full IDE behavior without first nailing the most-used 20%.
- **Open questions:** How far do we go on Arduino IDE parity before broadening into PlatformIO-class workflows? What belongs in-browser vs in a local helper?
- **Exit criteria:** A beginner can select a board, compile, understand failures, upload, open serial, and recover from common mistakes without leaving ProtoPulse.
- **Verification plan:** Run scripted compile/upload flows for at least AVR + ESP32, validate parsed diagnostics, and regression-test serial/session handoff.
- **Related docs:** `docs/arduino-ide-integration-spec.md`, `docs/arduino-ide-api-contracts.md`, `docs/plans/2026-03-13-c5-firmware-runtime-program.md`.

### Epic C — Cross-Tool Integration / “One Tool, Zero Context Switching”

- **Goal:** Make the architecture, schematic, breadboard, BOM, validation, export, and manufacturing surfaces behave like one coherent system.
- **Why it matters:** ProtoPulse’s core promise breaks whenever users have to manually re-enter data between views.
- **Complexity:** `C5` — Shared source-of-truth rules across the product are architecture-defining.
- **Scope included:** `BL-0558`, `BL-0563`-`BL-0566`, `BL-0568`, `BL-0571`-`BL-0586`.
- **Scope excluded for now:** Brand-new moonshots that do not first improve coherence between existing surfaces.
- **Dependencies:** Shared identifiers between domains, navigation/focus primitives, and clear source-of-truth rules for BOM/design/manufacturing data.
- **Risks:** Hidden data ownership conflicts, accidental overwrite behavior, and brittle synchronization if mapping rules stay implicit.
- **Open questions:** Which domain owns value/MPN truth after initial placement: schematic, BOM, or both? Which cross-tool actions should be automatic vs confirm-first?
- **Exit criteria:** Users can move across key workflows without retyping the same facts or manually reconciling divergent data.
- **Verification plan:** Multi-view scenario tests covering schematic→BOM→PCB→validation→export and snapshot restore cascades.
- **Related docs:** `docs/product-analysis-report.md`, `docs/product-analysis-checklist.md`.

### Epic D — Collaboration, Review, and Branching

- **Goal:** Evolve collaboration from shared presence into a trustworthy review/approval/branching system for real team use.
- **Why it matters:** Real shared hardware/design work needs approvals, restore points, ECOs, branches, and session trust, not just concurrent cursors.
- **Complexity:** `C5` — Touches tenancy, review, restore, auditability, and branch semantics.
- **Scope included:** `BL-0181`-`BL-0190`, `BL-0525`-`BL-0527`, `BL-0380`-`BL-0384`.
- **Scope excluded for now:** Marketplace/community social layers that do not improve core design collaboration.
- **Dependencies:** Firm session revalidation, auditability, role enforcement, and an opinionated branching/review model.
- **Risks:** Access-control mistakes, CRDT/branch semantics getting too clever, and approval flows that become too heavyweight for makers.
- **Open questions:** Should design branching be Git-like, snapshot-based, or review-first? What is the minimum viable ECO flow that still feels safe?
- **Exit criteria:** A team can branch, review, approve, restore, and audit meaningful design changes with clear ownership and history.
- **Verification plan:** Role-based integration tests, reconnect/auth tests, branch/merge scenario coverage, and audit-log traceability checks.
- **Related docs:** `docs/DEVELOPER.md`, `docs/backend-audit-checklist.md`, `docs/plans/2026-03-13-c5-collaboration-foundation-program.md`.

### Epic E — Manufacturing, BOM Intelligence, and Fab Readiness

- **Goal:** Make manufacturing outputs rely on actual project data and surface supply/fab risks before users commit.
- **Why it matters:** This is where “cool demo” turns into “real tool I trust before spending money.”
- **Complexity:** `C4` — Systemic integration across BOM, validation, export, and supplier/fab workflows.
- **Scope included:** `BL-0468`-`BL-0476`, `BL-0528`-`BL-0533`, `BL-0564`, `BL-0565`, `BL-0572`, `BL-0579`, `BL-0580`, `BL-0581`, `BL-0586`.
- **Scope excluded for now:** Marketplace-scale supply-chain features that do not directly improve project-level manufacturability.
- **Dependencies:** Real BOM integration, reliable file generation checks, supplier data strategy, and clear validation ownership.
- **Risks:** Mock supplier data undermining user trust, fab-specific API complexity, and false confidence from partial DFM coverage.
- **Open questions:** When should real-time supplier data be required vs optional? Should fab package export auto-create a frozen manufacturing snapshot?
- **Exit criteria:** A user can validate, package, price-check, and hand off a project for fab with materially less manual reconciliation.
- **Verification plan:** Fixture-based export validation, BOM-to-package integration tests, and DFM navigation/UX regression tests.
- **Related docs:** `docs/product-analysis-report.md`, `docs/app-audit-checklist.md`.

### Epic F — Learning, Starter Experience, and Guided Success

- **Goal:** Make ProtoPulse usable by someone who is still learning electronics, not just someone already fluent in EDA workflows.
- **Why it matters:** The target user is explicitly a maker/learner who needs momentum and guardrails, not just raw capability.
- **Complexity:** `C4` — Cross-surface onboarding and guidance work with a wide UX verification burden.
- **Scope included:** `BL-0300`-`BL-0314`, `BL-0627`, `BL-0628`, `BL-0460`.
- **Scope excluded for now:** Education platform expansion that depends on unresolved classroom or org-tenancy architecture.
- **Dependencies:** Starter content curation, role/beginner-mode framing, and consistent help/onboarding entry points across views.
- **Risks:** Overbuilding tutorial surfaces without reducing actual product friction, and guidance that drifts out of sync with real workflows.
- **Open questions:** Is the right top-level mode “Beginner / Hobbyist / Pro,” guided sample projects, or both? What should first-run success look like?
- **Exit criteria:** A new user can open a starter project, understand what to do next, and reach a working result without leaving the tool.
- **Verification plan:** First-run walkthrough tests, starter-project smoke tests, and UX review of progressive disclosure across core views.
- **Related docs:** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`, `docs/product-analysis-checklist.md`.

## Definition of Ready

- The item has a stable `BL-XXXX` ID and a clear source/cross-reference.
- The problem statement is user-visible or system-visible, not just a vague idea title.
- Dependencies or prerequisite decisions are named explicitly.
- Complexity is estimated if the item is larger than a trivial bugfix or copy change.
- Scope is small enough to fit in a wave, or the item is elevated into an epic with child IDs.
- Acceptance signal is obvious enough that another engineer could verify completion without guessing.
- High-risk items have at least one note about performance, security, or UX failure modes.

## Definition of Done

- Status is updated to `DONE` with the Wave number and verification detail available.
- The implemented workflow works end-to-end in the relevant surface, not just at the API or data-model layer.
- Tests or verification evidence exist at the right layer for the change.
- Any affected cross-tool links, navigation, or synchronization paths were checked for regressions.
- User-facing trust details are honest: no fake status, no hidden mock limitations, no silent failure path.
- Related backlog items were updated if scope changed, split, or made another row obsolete/duplicate.

## Complexity Scale

- **`C1` — Localized:** One component, one route, or one utility; little coordination risk.
- **`C2` — Multi-file / single surface:** Affects a contained workflow within one domain such as one editor, panel, or route family.
- **`C3` — Cross-surface:** Touches multiple layers such as UI + API + storage, or connects two existing domains.
- **`C4` — Systemic:** Cross-domain integration, new shared data contracts, or high verification burden across several workflows.
- **`C5` — Platform / architecture-defining:** New subsystem, runtime, tenancy model, execution environment, or a decision that many downstream items depend on.
- **Rule of thumb:** Effort asks "how much work is this right now?" Complexity asks "how many systems, decisions, and failure modes are tied up in this?"

## Planning Fields for New and Updated Items

- **User Impact:** `High`, `Medium`, or `Low`.
- **Effort:** `S`, `M`, `L`, or `XL` for expected implementation size.
- **Complexity:** `C1` to `C5` using the complexity scale below.
- **Confidence:** `High`, `Medium`, or `Low` based on how well-understood the work is.
- **Dependencies:** IDs, systems, or decisions that must land first.
- **Tags:** Use concise tags such as `security`, `simulation`, `arduino`, `collaboration`, `manufacturing`, `integration`, `frontend`, `backend`, `schema`, `tests`, `docs`, `ux`, `learning`, `performance`, `accessibility`, `ai`.
- **Verification Note:** Short note naming the expected proof, such as browser validation, integration test, export fixture, or hardware smoke test.

## Test / Verification Notes

- **UI / editor changes:** verify in a live browser and add or update regression tests where behavior is scriptable.
- **API / storage changes:** add route/storage coverage and confirm response-shape compatibility.
- **Cross-tool integration work:** test the full source→target workflow, not just one side of the bridge.
- **Simulation / analysis work:** use fixture circuits, numerical sanity checks, and runtime/performance guardrails.
- **Arduino / toolchain work:** parse real CLI output samples, test common failure cases, and verify serial/compile/upload state transitions.
- **Manufacturing / export work:** validate generated files, not just the button path that produces them.

## How to Add a New Item

- Add the item to the most relevant detailed backlog section below.
- Include the stable `BL-XXXX` ID, source reference, and initial status.
- For anything bigger than a single wave, also add or update an epic summary in the planning layer above.
- If the item is not truly ready to build, put it in `Discovery / Spikes`, `Blocked / Waiting On`, or `Decision Needed` as well.
- Add planning fields whenever possible: user impact, effort, complexity, confidence, dependencies, tags, and verification note.
- If the item changes scope or splits into sub-work, update the parent epic or nearby rows so nothing silently disappears.

### Complex Item Template

- **Goal:** What this work is trying to accomplish.
- **Why it matters:** Why the user or system should care.
- **Scope included:** What is in.
- **Scope excluded:** What is explicitly out for now.
- **Dependencies:** What must land first.
- **Risks:** What could go wrong.
- **Open questions:** What still needs answers.
- **Acceptance criteria:** What “good enough” means.
- **Verification plan:** How we will prove it works.
- **Related child items:** The `BL-XXXX` rows carrying the actual work.
- **Related docs:** Specs, audits, ADRs, or plans worth linking.

| BL-0909 | **Engine Gerber export omits soldermask/paste/silkscreen** — `packages/export/src/gerber.ts` only emits copper (`CopperLayer = 'F.Cu' | 'B.Cu'`, gerber.ts:23), FileFunction Copper L1/L2 (gerber.ts:140), and Profile/Edge.Cuts (gerber.ts:222) — no Mask/Paste/Legend layer anywhere in `packages/export/src/` (bom-csv, excellon, gerber, kicad-netlist, panel, pick-place, sexpr). `Footprint` (`packages/parts/src/types.ts:73-75`) is pads+courtyard only, so mask/silk geometry can't even be derived from current part data. ROADMAP.md:216 advertises the 'Fab outputs' panel as a full 'board fab set' but a fab given only copper+edge+drill produces bare copper with no legend/mask — JLC/PCBWay CAM will reject or ship unintended boards. Legacy already ships `server/export/gerber/{soldermask,silkscreen,paste,stroke-font}.ts`; this gap blocks the migration milestone (legacy's Gerber set is strictly richer). **DONE (2026-07-10):** added `exportSoldermaskLayer`/`exportPasteLayer`/`exportSilkscreenLayer` to `packages/export/src/gerber.ts` + a new `stroke-font.ts` (ported vector glyph table), all algorithm ported from the legacy exporter to integer-nm geometry. Correction to this row's own claim: the existing `courtyard: {wNm, hNm}` field turned out sufficient for a silkscreen body outline (a reasonable rectangle approximation) — mask/silk geometry was NOT actually blocked on Footprint data, just unimplemented. Soldermask clearance now sources from a new required `mask_expansion_nm` deck field (`content/decks/*.json`) rather than a guess. Wired into `ExportPanel`'s "Download fab set": 5 files → 11. Verified: 60/60 export tests (19 new), 394/394 app, 38/38 drc, 25/25 content, 29/29 golden fixtures untouched. | DONE (BL-0909 2026-07-10) | C4 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: competitive-core-eda) |

### EDA Domain Correctness (Omni-eval 2026-07-01)

| ID | Description | Status | Complexity | Source |

| BL-0912 | **Engine DRC never validates drill size or annular ring on through-hole footprint pads — only vias are checked** — `packages/drc/src/run.ts`'s `RULES` array (lines 493-500) runs `ruleViaGeometry` (224-248) against `ctx.graph.pcb.vias` only; `buildItems()` (153-203) turns THT pads into `pad` CopperItems used solely for clearance/unrouted/zone/edge checks, and `drillNm` never reaches a drill/annular-ring comparison for pads. A DIP-8, barrel-jack, or THT connector footprint with an out-of-spec drill/ring sails through `packages/cli check` clean today, discoverable only at the fab house. **DONE (2026-07-10):** added `ruleThtPadGeometry` (`packages/drc/src/run.ts`), the pad counterpart of `ruleViaGeometry`, reusing the existing `DRC-DRILL`/`DRC-ANNULAR` codes. Threaded through a new `CopperItem.drillNm` field rather than re-walking placements a second time. Rect pads use the shorter side for the ring formula (tightest dimension). Verified: 43/43 drc tests (5 new), 29/29 golden fixtures clean (incl. `traffic-light-555`'s NE555 DIP-8), 21/21 cli tests. | DONE (BL-0912 2026-07-10) | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: eda-domain) |
| BL-0913 | **Legacy and engine fab-capability decks disagree on the same manufacturer's minimum annular ring, and legacy's numbers contradict the sources it cites** — `shared/drc-templates.ts` sets OSHPark `minAnnularRing=0.178mm` (line 83) and PCBWay `minAnnularRing=0.1mm` (line 66), citing `docs.oshpark.com/services/` and `pcbway.com/capabilities.html`. Live-verified: OSHPark's own page states "Minimum Annular Ring: 5mil (0.127mm)" (matches engine's `content/decks/oshpark-2layer-standard.json` `min_annular_nm=127000`, contradicts legacy) and PCBWay states "Minimum Annular Ring Width: 0.15mm (6mil)" (matches engine's `min_annular_nm=150000`, contradicts legacy's looser 0.1mm). The legacy DFM checker — still the shipping product users order boards through — under-restricts PCBWay annular ring today, risking a rejected/reworked fab order, and cites a source page that doesn't contain the number it uses. **DONE (2026-07-10):** live-refetched every cited source page and corrected all 5 `shared/drc-templates.ts` templates: PCBWay 0.1→0.15mm, OSHPark 2-Layer 0.178→0.127mm, OSHPark 4-Layer 0.178→0.1016mm (the 4/6-layer page states 4mil, distinct from 2-layer's 5mil — legacy had both at the same wrong 0.178mm). Also found and fixed the same bug class in JLCPCB's templates (not in the original finding's scope but caught via the same live-verification pass): both JLCPCB 2-Layer and 4-Layer had `minAnnularRing=0.13mm` against a real absolute-minimum floor of 0.18mm (2-layer) / 0.15mm (multilayer) — fixed in both `shared/drc-templates.ts` and `content/decks/jlcpcb-2layer-standard.json` (`min_annular_nm` 130000→180000, `rev` bumped 2026-05→2026-07). Verified: content/drc test suites green, golden fixtures unaffected (no golden design routes annular-ring-marginal geometry). See **BL-1094** for a second, separate manufacturer-capability registry (`client/src/lib/manufacturer-rules.ts`) discovered during this pass with the same bug class but out of this fix's scope. | DONE (BL-0913 2026-07-10) | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: eda-domain) |
| BL-1094 | **A second, separate manufacturer fab-capability registry (`client/src/lib/manufacturer-rules.ts`) has the same wrong-numbers-and-broken-citations bug BL-0913 just fixed in `shared/drc-templates.ts`** — discovered live-verifying BL-0913's sources. This mil-unit-based registry (4 manufacturers: jlcpcb, pcbway, oshpark, eurocircuits — a superset of `drc-templates.ts`'s 5 templates) has: (1) JLCPCB `minAnnularRing=5mil` (0.127mm) vs the real absolute-minimum floor of ~7mil (0.18mm, 2-layer 1oz) — too loose; (2) PCBWay `minAnnularRing=5mil` (0.127mm) vs the real 0.15mm (~5.9mil) — too loose; (3) OSHPark `minAnnularRing=7mil` (0.178mm) cited to `docs.oshpark.com/design-tools/eagle/design-rules/` — **that URL 404s**, a dead citation (the working page, `docs.oshpark.com/services/`, states 5mil/0.127mm for 2-layer, 4mil/0.1016mm for 4-6 layer — this registry has no layer-count distinction, unlike `drc-templates.ts`'s split 2-Layer/4-Layer templates, so the correct single value is ambiguous by design); (4) EuroCircuits `minAnnularRing=7mil` cited to `eurocircuits.com/pcb-design-guidelines/` — **that page is an article-index page with no technical spec on it at all**, the real number needs sourcing from a specific linked article (unresolved). Needs: (a) decide whether this registry should collapse into `drc-templates.ts` (one source of truth) or stay separate with corrected values, (b) resolve the EuroCircuits number from a real source, (c) resolve the OSHPark layer-count ambiguity. | OPEN | C2 | BL-0913 follow-on 2026-07-10 |

### Code Health — Engine (Omni-eval 2026-07-01)

| ID | Description | Status | Complexity | Source |
|----|-------------|--------|------------|--------|
| BL-0919 | **packages/emu/src/esp32s3.ts is a 9,475-line file with one 6,800-line class (`Esp32s3Core`, :2658-EOF) plus 2,340 lines of register constants — split into per-peripheral MMIO modules and a pure crypto module before the next breadth campaign multiplies it.** Already showing monolith pathologies: duplicated SHA-256 compression (module-level `sha256BlockInto` :1069 vs private `sha256Compress` :7464, same FIPS 180-4 algorithm twice), the 13,408-line/240-`it` test file can't use vitest intra-file parallelism, and single-file `tsc` OOMs on this box, silently defeating `check:packages` verification (per memory note `feedback_emu_typecheck_verification_trap`). 159 commits touched this file since 2026-05-01. Extract pure crypto (AES/SHA blocks, zero `this`) to `esp32s3/crypto.ts` first — zero behavioral risk, kills the duplication — then hoist self-contained peripherals (I2C, SPI, MCPWM, TWAI, LEDC, TIMG, PCNT) behind an `MmioBlock` interface and address-dispatch table, and split the test file per peripheral. Directly de-risks the board/sensor breadth campaign (BL-0889 pattern) that keeps growing this file. | OPEN | C4 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: code-health-engine) |
| BL-0920 | **app's session dispatch does full op-log replay from genesis on every action, no snapshot fast-path, and discards the violations/warnings materialize() already computed** — `packages/app/src/state/session.ts:149,167,413` all call `materialize(this.log.opsFor(branch)).graph`, taking only `.graph` and dropping `.warnings`/`.violations`. `packages/graph/src/materialize.ts:30-31` defines `opts.from` ('start from a snapshot instead of the empty graph') and `materialize.test.ts:90-100` proves the snapshot+tail fast-path is mathematically identical to full replay — but session.ts never passes it, and the undo log has no cap, so replay cost grows unbounded. Wire `opts.from` off a periodic snapshot and surface the discarded violations/warnings instead of recomputing them elsewhere. | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: code-health-engine) |
| BL-0921 | **app hand-duplicates lazily-imported engine package type contracts (emu/sim/cosim/review) and bridges with `as unknown as`, invisibly defeating the compiler on every one of them.** `packages/app/src/emu/types.ts` re-declares `PinEvent`/`McuInspection`/`McuCore`/`EmuModule` that also live in `packages/emu/src/types.ts`, carrying a stale comment ('Partial in practice until the emulation track lands') though the track landed and was gate-certified 2026-06-23. Cast sites: `emu/runner.ts:34`, `sim/runner.ts:59`, `sim/ghost.ts:98`, `cosim/runner.ts:78`, `review/runner.ts:41` — all `(await import('@protopulse/X')) as unknown as Partial<XModule>`, accounting for 19 of app's 19 non-test `as unknown as` escapes vs 2 in the rest of the engine combined (rg, test files excluded). Since engine packages ship TS source, `import type` is erased at runtime, so switching to real type imports keeps the lazy-`import()` startup-cost rationale intact while letting the compiler catch real signature drift instead of silently passing it through to a runtime crash. Caveat: if the duplication exists to spare tsc from pulling 9.5k-line esp32s3.ts into app's program, sequence the esp32s3 split (see the esp32s3.ts monolith finding) first. | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: code-health-engine) |
| BL-0922 | **No shared footprint-source contract: route/app declare 3 duplicate structural interfaces while drc/renderer/export couple to concrete PartDb, bridged by 9 `as unknown as` casts at the geometry boundary.** Duplicate interfaces, all shaped `get(partId, rev) → { footprint? } | undefined`: `packages/route/src/obstacles.ts:48` `FootprintSource`, `packages/app/src/pcb/tools.ts:35` `PadSource`, `packages/app/src/pcb/selectors.ts:8` `NamedPadSource`. Meanwhile `drc/run.ts:19`, `renderer/scene-pcb.ts:11`, `export/gerber.ts:8` import concrete `PartDb` from `@protopulse/parts`. Bridge casts compile even if the shapes fully diverge: `CanvasHost.tsx:113,324`, `router-host.ts:28-29`, `DrcPanel.tsx:41`, `PcbTray.tsx:31`, `App.tsx:97`, `pcb/tools.ts:456,518`. Export one structural `FootprintSource` from `@protopulse/parts`, have `PartDb implements FootprintSource`, and consume the interface across route/drc/renderer/export/app — deletes all 9 casts and closes the one place where a shape drift means wrong copper. | OPEN | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: code-health-engine) |
| BL-0923 | **ERC pin-conflict matrix verdicts are mostly untested: unlisted pairs silently return OK, and only 'canonical conflicts' are asserted — the worst failure mode for an electrical rule checker.** `packages/erc/src/matrix.ts:80` `pinPairVerdict` falls through to `OK` for any pair absent from `UPPER`; deleting a row (e.g. `output×open_collector → OC_DRIVEN`) would change verdicts with zero test failures. `erc.test.ts` has 13 cases total (lowest density in the engine, 13/478 src lines) — one symmetry test (:41) and one canonical-conflicts test (:49). Codes like `ERC-BIDI-DRIVEN`, `ERC-TRISTATE-ON-RAIL`, `ERC-OC-ON-RAIL` have no dedicated assertion, and the matrix is shared by erc, drc, and review (`Finding` type). A single exhaustive table-driven test freezing all 55 unique pair verdicts against Vol II §C.1 is ~40 lines and doubles as living documentation. | OPEN | C1 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: code-health-engine) |

| BL-0924 | **Convert `check:packages` to composite TypeScript project references — root cause of the earlyoom/OOM typecheck trap** — `packages/tsconfig.json`/`tsconfig.base.json` set no `composite`, `references`, or `incremental`; `check:packages` (`package.json`) runs `tsc -p packages` as one flat program across all 16 engine packages (~68k TS). `.claudekit/config.json` already forces `NODE_OPTIONS=--max-old-space-size=16384` to cope, and Memory note `feedback_emu_typecheck_verification_trap.md` documents this exact command OOM-terminating and returning a false "0 errors" on Tyler's ~7GB box. Convert each `packages/*/tsconfig.json` to `composite: true` project references and run `tsc -b -p packages` so packages typecheck independently/incrementally instead of one 16GB all-or-nothing gamble every run. | OPEN | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: meta-layer) |

Add per-package `coverage.thresholds` mirroring graph's pattern (tuned per package, not copy-pasted), starting with the correctness-critical four: erc, drc, route, emu — a silent coverage regression on electrical/design rule checks or the ESP32-S3 emulator would currently ship undetected. (Related: BL-0265 "CI coverage gates and test quality thresholds," DONE Wave 113, covers the legacy client/server surface — this is the un-migrated engine-package gap.). Add a dedicated `e2e` CI job (`playwright install --with-deps chromium` + dev server + `test:e2e`, split a11y/keyboard-nav into parallel jobs given the 30s-per-test budget). | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: meta-layer) |

### Security & Robustness (Omni-eval 2026-07-01)

| ID | Description | Status | Complexity | Source |
|----|-------------|--------|------------|--------|
| BL-0927 | **`payloadLimit()` checks Content-Length after the body is already parsed by larger global limits — route-specific size caps are dead weight** — `server/routes/utils.ts:30-38`'s `payloadLimit(maxBytes)` reads `req.headers['content-length']`, but `registerRoutes(app)` (`server/index.ts:318`) runs after the global body parsers (`express.json` 1mb, `urlencoded` 1mb, `raw` 5mb, `text` 2mb registered at `server/index.ts:180-185`) — the body is already fully buffered by the time the route-level check runs. Absent Content-Length (e.g. chunked encoding), `undefined || '0'` always passes. Proof it's live: `server/routes/backup.ts:192` declares `payloadLimit(100 * 1024 * 1024)` for `/api/admin/restore`, but that body is parsed by the global 1MB `express.json` parser first — the 100MB limit is unreachable. 108 call sites across `server/routes/*.ts` and `server/circuit-routes/*.ts` rely on this broken pattern (verified via `rg -c payloadLimit\(`). | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: security-robustness) |
| BL-0928 | **`/api/admin/restore` executes arbitrary client-supplied SQL via psql, gated by an `ADMIN_API_KEY` with zero strength/presence validation** — `server/routes/backup.ts:189-265` pipes the request body's `sql` field directly into `psql -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1` stdin (lines 235-252), executing arbitrary SQL/DDL/DML (and potentially `COPY ... FROM PROGRAM` OS command execution) against production. The only gate is `requireAdminKey` → `safeCompareAdminKey`, which does reject on an empty/unset key (`if (!provided || !expected) return false`, `backup.ts:34-36` — so an unconfigured key closes the endpoint, not opens it). But `rg -n "ADMIN_API_KEY" server/env.ts` confirms zero length/format/production-required validation in `validateEnv()`, unlike `API_KEY_ENCRYPTION_KEY` which `server/auth.ts:53-74` strictly requires as 64-hex-char and throws in production if malformed. BL-0268 (DONE) only verified the comparison is timing-safe and rate-limited (5/min) — it never addressed that the secret itself can be arbitrarily weak (e.g. `admin`) with no startup warning, on a full-database-destructive endpoint. Cross-reference: BL-0268. | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: security-robustness) |
| BL-0929 | **Express `trust proxy` is hardcoded to 1 hop in all environments; the fully-built `TRUST_PROXY` config is dead code** — `server/index.ts:37-39` hardcodes `app.set("trust proxy", 1)` with a comment 'Replit runs behind one reverse proxy.' `server/lib/deployment-profiles.ts:293,298-325` fully implements a `TRUST_PROXY` env-var override with validation, but `rg -rn "getActiveProfile|deployment-profiles" server --type ts -g '!*.test.ts'` shows zero non-test importers — the module is entirely disconnected. Any deployment topology other than exactly-one-Replit-proxy (self-hosted, Cloudflare+LB=2 hops, dev without a proxy) lets an attacker spoof `X-Forwarded-For` to bypass IP-based rate limiting on auth/admin/AI endpoints, and the documented `TRUST_PROXY` env var has zero actual effect. | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: security-robustness) |
| BL-0930 | **packages/relay has no per-room authorization — a single optional global token gates ALL rooms, regressing legacy's per-connection project-ownership check** — `packages/relay/src/server.ts:159-164`'s only join-time check is `if (opts.token !== undefined && msg.token !== opts.token)`; any client presenting the one shared token may join ANY room by name, and `packages/relay/src/main.ts:14-26` shows the token is entirely optional (`PP_RELAY_TOKEN`), logging itself as 'open' when unset. Legacy's `server/collaboration.ts:189` `validateWsSession(sessionId, projectId)` validates a per-user session AND project ownership before admitting a WS client, with periodic revalidation. No existing backlog item covers `packages/relay` (confirmed via grep — zero hits). Currently dev/localhost-scoped, but the relay carries the live op-log for collaborative design editing and the roadmap is actively building toward non-localhost deployment (v0.5 Bridge / v0.6 World), where this becomes a real cross-user data-isolation gap. | OPEN | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: security-robustness) |
| BL-0931 | **packages/relay has no ceiling on total rooms or per-room op-log growth — only single-message caps exist** — `packages/relay/src/protocol.ts:26-28` defines only `MAX_BATCH_ENVELOPES=5000`, `MAX_MESSAGE_BYTES=8MB`, `MAX_ROOM_CHARS=128`; `packages/relay/src/server.ts:224-234`'s own comment states 'Empty rooms keep their log ... Memory is bounded by design size, not time,' but no code bounds `rooms.size` or a room's `BranchLog.log` Map, which only grows. A client sending repeated `ops` messages with unique `(actor, lamport)` envelopes can grow a room's in-memory log without limit. This runs on a box already known to have `earlyoom` kill the largest-RSS process under normal dev load (project memory: `project_earlyoom_kills_heavy_builds.md`) — an unauthenticated-by-default WS service with no room/log ceiling is a straightforward memory-exhaustion DoS vector. | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: security-robustness) |
| BL-0932 | **@protopulse/ai's destructive-tool confirmation gate is fail-open and unwired in every live panel** — `packages/ai/src/agent.ts:106-113`: `if (destructive && confirmDestructive)` skips the entire guard when `confirmDestructive` is undefined, and it's optional in every entry point (`agent.ts:35,157`, `router.ts:28`, `architect.ts:26`, `buyer.ts:28`). `rg -n "confirmDestructive" packages/app/src` returns zero matches, while RouterPanel.tsx:45, ArchitectPanel.tsx:43, BuyerPanel.tsx:46, AnalystPanel.tsx:55 all call their `run*` function without passing it — despite Router's `route_connection`/`remove_trace`, Architect's 3 tools, and Buyer's 1 tool all being marked `destructive: true` in their own registries. Ops are undoable (limits blast radius), but in a live multi-user collaboration session, an LLM-proposed destructive edit lands with zero confirmation and other participants may not notice before it propagates. DraftsmanPanel is currently a stub so its 7 destructive tools aren't live yet. | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: security-robustness) |
| BL-0933 | **Client-side SSO/OIDC library decodes id_token JWTs with zero signature verification** — `client/src/lib/auth/sso-oidc.ts:248-264`'s `decodeJwtPayload()` does `atob(payload)` + `JSON.parse()` only. Each of the 7 preset providers defines a `jwksUri` (lines 124-198), but `rg -n "jwks|verify|signature|jose|jsonwebtoken"` shows it's never fetched or used; the decoded payload feeds group-to-role mapping at lines 550/610. The module is orphaned today — `rg -n "decodeJwtPayload|sso-oidc" client/src -l` returns only its own test file, and `server` has zero id_token/SSO consumption. BL-0380 (DONE, 105 tests) documents this as shipped with 'group-to-role mapping,' overstating its readiness. Dormant landmine now, but the moment a real SSO button is wired to this library, any attacker able to influence the id_token can self-assign arbitrary roles/groups since nothing checks the issuer signature. Cross-reference: BL-0380. | OPEN | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: security-robustness) |

Not a true duplicate of BL-0870 (that item is scoped to the legacy Three.js `viewer_3d` 3D-board tab, a different canvas/surface entirely). | OPEN | C4 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: a11y-static) |

| ID | Description | Status | Complexity | Source |

| BL-0935 | **Offline write queue never wired into real mutations — offline edits are lost, not queued** — `client/src/lib/offline-sync.ts` implements a real IndexedDB-backed offline mutation queue (`interceptMutation()` lines 153-176, exponential-backoff sync w/ 409/ETag conflict detection) but `rg -n "interceptMutation|OfflineSyncManager|useOfflineSync" client/src` matches only inside offline-sync.ts and its own tests — zero call sites in any domain context (bom-context, architecture-context, project-meta-context, etc.) or component. `client/src/lib/queryClient.ts:107-129` configures `mutations.retry: false` with an `onError` that only toasts (no persister, no networkMode, no link to the queue), so a failed mutation while offline is silently discarded instead of queued for later sync. Cross-reference: BL-0527. | OPEN | C4 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: critic:offline-pwa-story) |

The restore route captures pre/post `pg_stat_user_tables` row counts as an "audit trail," which proves *a* restore ran, not that it round-tripped correct data or didn't silently truncate/corrupt tables. | OPEN | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: critic:backup-disaster-recovery) |
| BL-0937 | **Legacy schematic/architecture editor has no local crash-recovery autosave — only the new engine editor does** — `packages/app/src/state/persistence.ts:65-99` (`createAutosave`, 1s debounce, `localStorage` key `pp-design`) is wired in `packages/app/src/main.tsx:48-53` with a `beforeunload` flush. Legacy's `client/src/hooks/useSyncedFlowState.ts` only debounce-flushes ReactFlow state back into the in-memory `ProjectProvider` context (lines 73-115) — not browser storage, so it doesn't survive a tab crash or OOM kill. `rg -il "autosave|crash.recover" client/src` finds no localStorage/IndexedDB recovery mechanism for the schematic/architecture surface. On Tyler's ~7GB dev box, where earlyoom SIGTERMs the highest-RSS process, a crashed legacy editor tab loses all edits since the last server mutation with no recovery snapshot. | OPEN | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: critic:backup-disaster-recovery) |

### Data Format & Schema-Versioning (Omni-eval 2026-07-01)

| ID | Description | Status | Complexity | Source |
|----|-------------|--------|------------|--------|
| BL-0938 | **Browser autosave silently discards the user's design on any bundle-shape mismatch, with just a console.warn** — `packages/app/src/state/persistence.ts:53-63` `loadBundle()`: any `decodeBundle` zod validation failure is caught, logged via `console.warn('saved design is unreadable — starting fresh', err)`, and returns `null` — the caller then boots `loadFixture()` (`persistence.ts:125-133`), an empty starter design. This isn't only a future-migration risk: any localStorage corruption today — truncated write, cross-tab race, manual edit — hits this same silent-discard path with no user-facing error, no raw-text backup, and no recovery UI. Currently affects the dev-preview engine editor (packages/app), not the shipping legacy product. | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: critic:ppx-schema-versioning-migration) |

### Security / Dependency Vulnerabilities (2026-06-29 — `/project-metrics` security pass)

| ID | Description | Status | Complexity | Source |
|----|-------------|--------|------------|--------|
| BL-0907 | **5 unpatchable HIGH vulns in shipped `genkit` / `@opentelemetry` chain** — `npm audit` reports HIGH advisories on `@genkit-ai/core`, `@genkit-ai/firebase`, `@genkit-ai/google-cloud`, `@opentelemetry/sdk-node`, `@opentelemetry/auto-instrumentations-node`, all `fixAvailable=false` (no published patched release). This chain ships at runtime — `server/genkit.ts`, `server/ai.ts`, `server/circuit-ai/{generate,analyze,review}.ts`, `server/batch-analysis.ts` all import `genkit` / `@genkit-ai/google-genai`. **Reachability nuance:** the app uses the `@genkit-ai/google-genai` provider, NOT firebase/google-cloud directly — the vulnerable transitives are genkit's *telemetry/firebase* sub-deps and may not be on the active code path (verify before deciding severity-in-practice). **Remediation options, in order of preference:** (a) wait for + bump to an upstream `genkit` release that bumps the vulnerable transitives; (b) `overrides` pin the patched `@opentelemetry/*` (and offending `@genkit-ai/*`) versions in root `package.json` — risky, may break genkit, REQUIRES a green server test run after; (c) drop unused genkit sub-packages / disable telemetry if firebase/google-cloud paths are confirmed dead. NOT a blind bump — needs a real research pass + tested change. Companion to the 2026-06-29 toolchain bump (commit `1ade7242`) that already cleared the 2 critical + 2 high *fixable* vulns (vitest/coverage/vite/undici). Effectively BLOCKED-on-upstream for option (a). **Upstream check 2026-07-01:** genkit 1.39.0 released (we run 1.37.0) but `@genkit-ai/core@1.39.0` pins the IDENTICAL otel versions (`~1.25.0`/`^0.52.0`) — chain still vulnerable upstream; option (a) remains blocked. Next check: on the next genkit minor, diff `npm view @genkit-ai/core@<ver> dependencies` otel pins first. | OPEN | C3 | /project-metrics 2026-06-29 |

### Ledger Integrity (2026-07-01 — first deterministic recount)

| ID | Description | Status | Complexity | Source |
|----|-------------|--------|------------|--------|
| BL-0908 | **Reconcile MASTER_BACKLOG tables with Quick Stats — 158 OPEN rows vs "1 open" claim** — The first deterministic recount (`.claude/skills/bl/scripts/recount.py`, built 2026-07-01) proved the ledger internally inconsistent: Quick Stats said 1 open / 513 done while the P0-P3 tables actually hold **158 OPEN + 544 DONE rows (702 items; 713 unique BL ids doc-wide)**. The counter is validated — P0 (0/19) and P2 (0/285) match Quick Stats EXACTLY; the drift is localized: (a) the two "Frontend E2E Walkthrough 2026-04-18 (batch triage)" tables in P1 (~186 rows: 155 OPEN + ~31 DONE) never entered Quick Stats arithmetic at all — the wave-closure updates changed the stats block by decree without flipping rows, and later snapshots inherited the fiction; (b) 2 P3 OPEN stragglers; (c) BL-0711 pair under Developer Platform. **Reconciliation = per-row truth-finding, not bulk-flipping:** many E2E items are likely REAL open bugs that were statistically vanished (the 399-finding breadboard audit's Waves 2+ are known-open) — cross-reference each OPEN row against wave history/git/`docs/audits/`, flip the provably-done, keep the genuinely-open visible, then regenerate Quick Stats from `recount.py --json` output and fix the "514 items tracked" fiction (real: 713 ids). Until then Quick Stats numbers are UNRELIABLE — recount.py is the source of truth. **Correction (2026-07-02):** this row's original hypothesis that "BL-0773/0790/0850 were closed by the BL-0781 wave with rows never flipped" was FALSIFIED — the BL-0781 wave had never actually run (grep-verified: only 3 pre-existing NumberInput adopters, 66 raw `type="number"` instances still present). The wave was executed and committed today (`571a9438`), and BL-0773/0790/0850 are now genuinely DONE-BY-BL-0781, not retroactively-stale. This BL-0908 row itself remains open — the other ~155 pre-existing E2E-cohort rows still need the same per-row truth-finding, this was just the one hypothesis that happened to get tested. **DONE (2026-07-10) — full reconciliation complete.** All three open threads (a)/(b)/(c) closed: **(a) E2E cohort (151 rows, not 155 — a few had already been closed by earlier waves):** 6 agents worked in parallel, each verifying a ~25-row batch against live code/git history (one agent instance never delivered results after 5 attempts across two spawns; its 19-row batch was verified directly instead). Every row got an evidence-backed verdict — 52 DONE, 3 OBSOLETE (one a malformed ingestion artifact with no real content, BL-0842 — kept in the ledger with an explanatory note rather than silently deleted), 96 reconfirmed genuinely open (85 STILL_OPEN, 10 UNCERTAIN kept open conservatively since no mechanical evidence could resolve them, 1 duplicate pair BL-0757/BL-0765 both open). Every DONE/OBSOLETE row now carries inline evidence (file:line or commit sha) instead of a bare status flip. **(b) 2 P3 stragglers:** already resolved by an earlier correction pass — confirmed via zero P3 drift in this pass, no action needed. **(c) BL-0711 pair:** false alarm — BL-0711/BL-0712 are two legitimately distinct, correctly-filed items under Developer Platform, not an ID collision; `recount.py` confirms zero duplication there. **Bonus finding:** a REAL, different ID collision was caught in the process — BL-0902 was independently assigned to two unrelated items (the AI-eval-harness, DONE, deeply cross-referenced across 15+ `packages/ai/evals/*` files + its own plan doc filename; and a `server/` refactor item, OPEN, zero code references). Worse: `recount.py`'s duplicate-handling was *silently skipping* the second occurrence from every count (not just flagging it) — meaning the refactor item's OPEN status had been invisible to P2's tally since 2026-06-27, undercounting P2 open by 1 the entire time. Renumbered the refactor item to **BL-1093**, fixed `recount.py` to formally recognize the new `OBSOLETE` status (added for BL-0842/BL-0729/BL-0871) so it doesn't get silently dropped the same way. Quick Stats regenerated from a clean `recount.py` run: zero duplicates, zero unclassified rows, P1 128/166, P2 122/285 (true count), P3 35/133, Total 285/603 open/done (899 unique ids). | DONE (BL-0908 reconciliation 2026-07-10) | C3 | /bl recount 2026-07-01 |

| BL-0858 | **Plan↔Vault slug name drift** — 2026-04-19 session-7 audit on 14 Vault Integration subsections: only 11/93 (12%) cited slugs resolve exactly to a `knowledge/<slug>.md` file. Most misses are (a) legitimate seed gaps awaiting `/vault-gap` + `/extract` (expected), but (b) ~5-10 are NAME DRIFT: plan cites `wcag-2-1-sc-1-4-1-color-cannot-be-sole-channel` but extract produced `...-for-meaning` suffix; plan cites `awg-vs-mm2-wire-sizing` vs extracted `awg-vs-mm2-wire-sizing-logarithmic-conversion`. Fix options: (1) ship fuzzy-matcher that suggests corrections + auto-updates plan subsections; (2) add `aliases: []` frontmatter to notes so short-form slugs resolve; (3) teach `/extract` to emit the plan's requested slug verbatim when it's provided in the origin stub. Option 3 is most robust long-term. | OPEN | C2 | Slug-resolution audit 2026-04-19 (93 cited, 11 resolved) |

| BL-0881 | **NumberInput exponential-notation precision loss for ultra-small values** — `client/src/components/ui/number-input.tsx#formatAriaNumber` (added in BL-0880) uses `value.toLocaleString('en-US', { useGrouping: false, maximumFractionDigits: 20 })` to render `aria-valuenow` without exponential notation (axe fails `aria-valid-attr-value` on `1e-9` etc.). Codex flagged in BL-0879 Round 1 self-audit: **`1e-21` formats to `'0'`** under `maximumFractionDigits: 20`, silently losing the value. Calculator domain values (RC time constants, capacitor pF reactances) can plausibly reach this range. **Fix candidates:** (a) bump `maximumFractionDigits` to `30` (max allowed in Intl spec) — covers down to `~1e-30`; (b) detect exponent and use a custom decimal-string serializer for sub-`1e-20` values; (c) treat `aria-valuenow` as optional and OMIT for values below precision threshold (verify WAI-ARIA conformance for input[type=number] without `aria-valuenow`). | OPEN | C3 | Codex flagged 2026-05-09 during BL-0879 Round 1 self-audit |

Need to verify `operationEntityKey` covers the insert case (currently returns `null` per `shared/collaboration.ts:81`). | BL-0883 | **state-sync needs document snapshot + replay + ACK for cold-start causal correctness (BL-0879 follow-up)** — Codex's BL-0879 Round 2 review flagged that the existing `state-sync` payload at `server/collaboration.ts:421-445` carries only `users`, `version`, `locks` — no document snapshot, no op replay, no client ACK. This means a fresh-connect or reconnecting client has no truthful baseline for its observed-frontier. BL-0879's Option C-plus-ACK plan accepts initializing observed-frontier to `0` for new connections (with possibly false-conflicts on first ops) until BL-0883 lands. Scope: extend `state-sync` payload with authoritative document state OR `op-replay` of the latest N ops with their server timestamps; client applies replay then sends `state-sync-ack` with the highest applied Lamport per touched key; server initializes `observedFrontiers[connectionId]` from the ACK, not the initial send. Requires deciding how to materialize "authoritative document state" (probably leverage existing project persistence / Drizzle snapshot). | OPEN | C2 | Codex flagged 2026-05-09 during BL-0879 Round 2 review |
| BL-0884 | **Per-connection outbound ACK ledger for spoofing-proof CRDT integrity (BL-0879 future)** — Codex flagged in BL-0879 Round 2 + reaffirmed Round 7 self-critique #2: the converged Option C-plus-ACK plan's server validates ACK timestamps via `ackedTs ≤ frontier.ts` clamp, which limits damage but does NOT verify the ACKed op was actually sent to that connection. A malicious client could ACK ops it never received to escape conflict detection. ProtoPulse's threat model is "honest collaborators" so this is acceptable for BL-0879 — but if/when ProtoPulse adds adversarial collaboration scenarios (public projects, untrusted contributors, audit-trail compliance), a per-connection outbound ledger is required. Scope: server tracks `outboundLedger: Map<connectionId, Map<entityKey, Set<timestamp>>>` recording every state-update or conflict-detected sent to each connection; ACK validation requires `ackedTs ∈ outboundLedger[C][K]`; ledger entries pruned on receipt or after grace window. Trade-off: extra memory proportional to (connections × pending-ACK ops). Defer until threat model changes; document as known limitation in BL-0879 release notes. | OPEN | C5 | Codex flagged 2026-05-09 during BL-0879 Round 7 self-critique |

| BL-0889 | **First breadth slice: TMP36 analog temp sensor → ADC co-sim — DONE 2026-06-23** — First slice of the post-gate "breadth" campaign (ROADMAP §v0.5, after the ESP32-S3 COMPLETION GATE certified 2026-06-23). Extends the certified ADC closed loop (`packages/cosim/src/quantum.esp32s3.cosim.test.ts`). Scope confirmed by Explore agent: **no new abstraction needed** — a sensor emits a behavioral SPICE B-source, the same pattern as the NE555 macromodel at `packages/sim/src/models.ts:234-272`. The TMP36 part already exists (`core:tmp36`) and is **vault-verified** (Analog Devices TMP35/36/37 datasheet Rev H: `Vout = 10mV/°C × T + 500mV`; pins VS/VOUT/GND; `inbox/2026-06-20-tmp36-pinout.md`). **Build (TDD):** (1) add `emitTmp36` to `packages/sim/src/models.ts` emitting a B-source with the transfer fn, temperature carried as a `temperature_C` **parametric on the placed part** (default 25°C — this models the sensor properly without inventing a new co-sim binding type; do NOT hardcode the voltage); (2) register `['core:tmp36', emitTmp36]` in `EMITTERS_BY_PART_ID` (`models.ts:299-315`); (3) new `packages/cosim/src/quantum.tmp36.cosim.test.ts` mirroring the divider KAT — supply 3.3 V, 25°C → 750 mV, real `Esp32s3Core` + `esp32s3-adc0-read.bin` through `runCosimClosedLoop`, settled reads track 750 mV ±20 mV, ESP32-S3 12-bit code = round(750/3300×4095) = **928**; (4) add `'core:tmp36'` to the verified-parts assertion in `packages/parts/src/parts.test.ts`. **Verify:** `npm run -w @protopulse/cosim test` + `npm run check:packages` clean. **Design choice (as built):** temperature is a settable part *value* — there is no per-instance parametrics map in the emitter path (`component.value` is the only channel), so the emitter parses `value` as °C (default 25), NOT a hardcoded voltage. Still a real sensor model, no `AdcBinding` schema change. **LANDED 2026-06-23 (merge `a846dd7c`):** `emitTmp36` in `packages/sim/src/models.ts` (ideal DC source VOUT→GND = 0.01·T + 0.5) + `packages/cosim/src/quantum.tmp36.cosim.test.ts` (5 tests, TDD red→green): real `runCosimClosedLoop`, MID solves **0.75 V @ 25°C / 1.00 V @ 50°C**, settled ADC reads track ±0.005 V, UART code = round(Vout/3.3·4095); the 50°C case proves a real transfer function, not a constant. Verified on main: **cosim 74/74**; targeted `tsc` sim+cosim **EXIT=0** (check:packages OOM-trap → per-package fallback, [[feedback_emu_typecheck_verification_trap]]). | DONE | C2 | Built 2026-06-23 — first breadth slice |
| BL-0890 | **Breadth slice 2: potentiometer (`core:pot-10k`) → ADC co-sim — DONE 2026-06-23** — Second breadth slice; generalizes the BL-0889 analog-ADC pattern from a 2-pin source (TMP36) to a **3-terminal voltage divider**. `emitPot10k` (`packages/sim/src/models.ts`) models the 10 kΩ pot as two resistors A–WIPER = 10k·pos / WIPER–B = 10k·(1−pos), wiper fraction `pos` on the part's `value` (default 0.5, clamped, 1 Ω floor). Test `packages/cosim/src/quantum.pot.cosim.test.ts` (5 tests, TDD red→green): VCC→A, WIPER→ADC0, B→GND → `V_wiper = 3.3·(1−pos)`; real `runCosimClosedLoop` + `Esp32s3Core` + `esp32s3-adc0-read.bin`; MID solves **2.31 V @ pos 0.3 / 0.99 V @ pos 0.7**, settled ADC reads track ±0.005 V, UART code = round(V/3.3·4095). pos=0.7 ≠ pos=0.3 proves a variable input, not a constant. **Verified on main:** cosim **79/79**; targeted `tsc` sim+cosim **EXIT=0**. **Note:** `value` carries wiper position (not resistance) for pots — resistance is fixed by the part; documented in the emitter. | DONE | C2 | Built 2026-06-23 — second breadth slice |
| BL-0891 | **Breadth slice 3: FC-28 soil moisture (`core:soil-moisture`) → ADC co-sim — DONE 2026-06-23** — Third breadth slice; first to model a **supply-dependent** analog output via a SPICE B-source (slices 1–2 used fixed DC sources). `emitSoilMoisture` (`packages/sim/src/models.ts`) models the FC-28 AO (pin 4) as `AO = (v(VCC)−v(GND))·(1−moisture)` referenced to GND — **web-verified direction** (dry→high ≈VCC, wet→low ≈0; sources: artofcircuits, maker.pro). Moisture fraction (0=dry…1=wet, default 0.5) on the part's `value`; simplified linear stand-in for the real non-linear curve, digital DO not modeled. Test `packages/cosim/src/quantum.soil.cosim.test.ts` (5 tests, TDD red→green): VCC→soil, AO→ADC0; real `runCosimClosedLoop`; MID solves **2.475 V @ moisture 0.25 / 0.825 V @ 0.75**, settled ADC reads track ±0.005 V, UART code = round(V/3.3·4095), `wet < dry` proves moisture drives the reading. **Verified on main:** cosim **84/84**; targeted `tsc` sim+cosim **EXIT=0**. | DONE | C2 | Built 2026-06-23 — third breadth slice |
| BL-0892 | **I²C slave-device hook — foundation for I²C digital-sensor co-sim — DONE 2026-06-23** — Unblocks the next breadth class (I²C digital sensors: BME280, GY-521/MPU-6050) which exchange register bytes over the bus rather than sourcing a voltage. The ESP32-S3 emu I²C master previously filled RX-FIFO reads with hardcoded zeros (`esp32s3.ts` i2cRunTransaction READ case). Added `Esp32s3Core.setI2cSlave(port, fn: (address, register) => byte)`: the master transaction recovers the 7-bit device address + register pointer from the bytes written this transaction (write `[addr+W, reg]` → restart → write `[addr+R]` → read), and calls the slave per read byte (register auto-increments on a burst). `null` removes it; **with no slave, reads still return 0 (no regression).** New emu test: a BME280-style slave at 0x76 answers chip-ID reg 0xD0 with 0x60 through a real master transaction. **Verified on main:** emu **326/326** (239 ESP32-S3); targeted `tsc` emu **EXIT=0**. **Follow-ups:** (a) a part→I²C-device-model registry (declare a part's address + register map), (b) a cosim bus-device binding (parametrized register values driven by cosim config, like the ADC sampler), (c) a first real device slice (BME280 temp/humidity read). | DONE | C2 | Built 2026-06-23 — I²C foundation |
| BL-0893 | **First real I²C device: GY-521/MPU-6050 IMU (`core:mpu6050`) — DONE 2026-06-24** — Builds on the BL-0892 slave hook (follow-up (c), done with MPU-6050 not BME280 — the MPU's registers are direct int16, no calibration-compensation math). New `packages/emu/src/i2c-devices.ts`: `i2cRegisterDevice(map)` builds an `I2cSlave` from a register map (absent regs → 0); `mpu6050(state)` models the MPU-6050 at addr 0x68 — WHO_AM_I 0x75=0x68, PWR_MGMT_1 0x6B=0x40 (SLEEP reset default), big-endian int16 ACCEL/GYRO from 0x3B (register map web-verified vs InvenSense RM + kriswiner). Exported from the emu package index. Test `i2c-devices.test.ts` (3 tests): firmware reads WHO_AM_I→0x68 and 2-byte burst-reads ACCEL_XOUT_H/L→0x12/0x34 (accelX=0x1234), proving register auto-increment; unset axes→0. **Verified on main:** emu **329/329**; targeted `tsc` emu (incl. package index) **EXIT=0**. **Remaining I²C follow-up:** a cosim bus-device binding (parametrized register values driven by cosim config, like the ADC sampler) + a part→device-model registry mapping `core:mpu6050`/`core:bme280` to their models. | DONE | C2 | Built 2026-06-24 — first real I²C device |
| BL-0894 | **I²C device in the co-sim closed loop — DONE 2026-06-24** — Completes the I²C sensor path (the remaining BL-0893 follow-up). Adds `ClosedLoopSpec.i2cDevices: { port: 0\|1, slave }[]` (`@protopulse/cosim` `types.ts`) — the bus-device counterpart of the ADC sampler. `runCosimClosedLoop` (`quantum.ts`) probes the pinned I²C surface (`hasI2cSupport`/`I2cCapableCore` → `setI2cSlave`, mirroring `hasAdcSupport`/`AdcCapableCore`) and installs each device before stepping. Test `quantum.i2c.cosim.test.ts`: a real `Esp32s3Core` runs I²C WHO_AM_I firmware through `runCosimClosedLoop` with an `mpu6050()` device on port 0 → reads 0x68. **Verified on main:** cosim **85/85**; targeted `tsc` cosim **EXIT=0**. **Remaining (optional):** per-quantum dynamic/parametrized device registers (devices are currently installed static for the run) + a part→device-model registry so a placed `core:mpu6050` auto-installs its model. | DONE | C2 | Built 2026-06-24 — I²C in co-sim |
| BL-0895 | **BME280 I²C device + part→device registry — DONE 2026-06-24** — A 2nd modeled I²C device + the connective registry. `bme280(state)` (`packages/emu/src/i2c-devices.ts`): Bosch BME280 (addr 0x76/0x77) — chip-ID 0xD0=0x60 (canonical identity), reset/status, 20-bit press/temp + 16-bit hum raw registers MSB-first (compensation firmware-side; calibration regs not modeled). `I2C_DEVICES_BY_PART_ID` + `i2cDeviceForPart(partId)`: maps `core:mpu6050`/`core:bme280` → device-model factories (the bus-device parallel to the analog `EMITTERS_BY_PART_ID`), so co-sim/app can auto-resolve a placed I²C part's model. Exported from the emu index. Tests: BME280 chip-ID read→0x60, registry lookup (mpu/bme resolve, non-I²C part→null). **Verified on main:** emu **331/331**; targeted `tsc` emu (incl. index) **EXIT=0**. **Remaining (optional):** wire `i2cDeviceForPart` into the co-sim so placed I²C parts auto-install (graph-scan for the part + its controller port) + per-quantum dynamic device registers. | DONE | C2 | Built 2026-06-24 — BME280 + I²C registry |
| BL-0896 | **Auto-resolve I²C devices from the design graph — DONE 2026-06-24** — Closes the BL-0895 "wire into co-sim" follow-up. `i2cDevicesFromGraph(graph)` (`packages/cosim/src/i2c-graph.ts`) scans `DesignGraph.components`, resolves each via the emu registry (`i2cDeviceForPart`), and returns `ClosedLoopSpec.i2cDevices` bindings (port 0 — the ESP32-S3 muxes I²C over the GPIO matrix, so the controller is firmware-decided not graph-encoded; DNP parts skipped). A caller feeds it to `runCosimClosedLoop` without hand-listing sensors. Exported from the cosim index. Tests: MPU+BME placed → 2 port-0 bindings answering their identity registers; no-I²C design → []. **Verified on main:** cosim **87/87**; targeted `tsc` cosim **EXIT=0**. Completes the I²C path end to end: emu master+slave hook (BL-0892) → device models (BL-0893/0895) → registry (BL-0895) → co-sim binding (BL-0894) → graph auto-resolve (this). **Remaining (optional):** per-quantum dynamic device registers (sensor values that change over the run / couple to a SPICE node). | DONE | C2 | Built 2026-06-24 — I²C graph auto-resolve |
| BL-0897 | **SPI slave-device hook (foundation for SPI device co-sim) — DONE 2026-06-24** — The SPI counterpart of the BL-0892 I²C hook, opening the next breadth class (SPI devices: RC522 RFID, MAX7219, SPI flash). The emu SPI master (`spiRunTransaction`) filled MISO reads with zeros — no way for a device to answer. Added `Esp32s3Core.setSpiSlave(port: 2\|3, fn: (mosi, misoIndex) => byte)`: the transaction collects the command/address/MOSI bytes clocked out (into `mosi[]`, still recorded in the host write-log) and the MISO phase calls the slave per read byte. `null` removes it; **no slave → MISO reads 0 (no regression).** New `SpiSlave` type exported. Test: a GPSPI2 slave feeds `[0xAB,0xCD]` into a 16-bit MISO read. **Verified on main:** emu esp32s3 **240/240**; targeted `tsc` emu (incl. index) **EXIT=0** (rp2040 full-suite timeout = load flake, 7/7 alone). **Follow-ups (mirror I²C):** SPI device models (e.g. RC522, an SPI-flash JEDEC-ID device) → registry → co-sim binding. | DONE | C2 | Built 2026-06-24 — SPI foundation |
| BL-0898 | **First SPI device: MFRC522/RC522 RFID reader (`core:rc522`) + SPI device registry — DONE 2026-06-24** — The SPI parallel to the I²C MPU-6050/registry slices (BL-0893/0895). `rc522(state)` (`packages/emu/src/spi-devices.ts`) models the NXP MFRC522 as an SPI slave: VersionReg 0x37 = 0x92 (v2.0, canonical identity check), decoding the read address byte `((reg<<1)&0x7E)|0x80` from `mosi[0]` (MFRC522 datasheet §8.1.2.3, web-verified); configurable version (0x91 v1.0 clone). `SPI_DEVICES_BY_PART_ID` + `spiDeviceForPart(partId)` map `core:rc522` → its model (SPI parallel to `I2C_DEVICES_BY_PART_ID`). Exported from the emu index. Test `spi-devices.test.ts` (3 tests): firmware reads VersionReg via a command(0xEE)+MISO transaction → 0x92 (and a configured 0x91); registry lookup resolves, non-SPI part → null. **Verified on main:** emu **335/335**; targeted `tsc` emu (incl. index) **EXIT=0**. **Follow-ups (mirror I²C):** a co-sim SPI-device binding (`ClosedLoopSpec.spiDevices`) + `spiDevicesFromGraph` graph auto-resolve. | DONE | C2 | Built 2026-06-24 — first SPI device |
| BL-0899 | **SPI device co-sim binding + graph auto-resolve (SPI at I²C parity) — DONE 2026-06-24** — Mirrors the I²C cosim binding (BL-0894/0896) for SPI, closing the BL-0898 follow-ups. `ClosedLoopSpec.spiDevices: { port: 2\|3, slave }[]` + `SpiCapableCore`/`hasSpiSupport`/`SpiDeviceBinding` (`@protopulse/cosim` `types.ts`); `runCosimClosedLoop` probes `setSpiSlave` and installs each device before stepping. `spiDevicesFromGraph(graph)` (`spi-graph.ts`) resolves placed SPI parts via `spiDeviceForPart` to port-2 (GPSPI2) bindings. Emu index now exports `ESP32S3_SPI2_BASE`/`ESP32S3_SPI3_BASE`. Tests: firmware reads RC522 VersionReg → 0x92 through `runCosimClosedLoop` with `rc522()` on port 2; `spiDevicesFromGraph` resolves `core:rc522`, non-SPI → []. **Verified on main:** cosim **90/90**; targeted `tsc` cosim+emu **EXIT=0**. **The SPI path is now end-to-end at I²C parity** (hook → device+registry → cosim binding → graph). Both digital-bus verticals complete. | DONE | C2 | Built 2026-06-24 — SPI at I²C parity |
| BL-0900 | **DS3231 RTC I²C device model (`core:ds3231`) — DONE 2026-06-24** — Third I²C device (an RTC), growing the simulatable catalog on the established registry pattern. `ds3231(state)` (`packages/emu/src/i2c-devices.ts`): Maxim DS3231 (addr 0x68) BCD timekeeping registers per datasheet §8.1 — 0x00 sec, 0x01 min, 0x02 hr (24h), 0x03 day, 0x04 date, 0x05 month, 0x06 year; state is decimal, BCD-encoded via `toBcd`. Registered in `I2C_DEVICES_BY_PART_ID`, exported from the emu index. Test: firmware burst-reads 0x00..0x02 for 13:30:42 → BCD `[0x42,0x30,0x13]`; registry resolves `core:ds3231`. **Verified on main:** emu **337/337**; `tsc` emu (incl. index) **EXIT=0**. **Catalog now:** I²C MPU-6050/BME280/DS3231, SPI RC522 — more devices are register-map + registry entries. | DONE | C3 | Built 2026-06-24 — DS3231 RTC device |
| BL-0901 | **Co-sim in the editor auto-installs device models for placed I²C/SPI parts — DONE 2026-06-24** — The product payoff of the device layer (BL-0892→0900): place a modeled I²C/SPI sensor and run a closed-loop co-sim, its device model auto-installs with zero wiring. `runClosedLoop` (`packages/app/src/cosim/runner.ts`) resolves `spec.i2cDevices`/`spiDevices` via the lazily-loaded cosim module's `i2cDevicesFromGraph`/`spiDevicesFromGraph` when omitted (`??` short-circuits → explicit list wins, graph resolution skipped); helpers probed (optional) for older builds. App cosim types gained `I2c`/`SpiDeviceBinding` + spec/module fields (local pinned-API mirror). Tests (`runner.test.ts`): auto-resolve from graph into the spec; explicit `i2cDevices` not overridden + graph resolution skipped. **Verified on main:** app `cosim/runner` **19/19**; app `tsc` **EXIT=0**. | DONE | C2 | Built 2026-06-24 — co-sim device auto-install in app |

Per-test fetch mocks still work via `vi.spyOn` overrides. **DONE 2026-05-09 Codex verification:** sentinel command logged at `logs/tests-bl0876-verify-pretest.log` passed **73/73 tests across 3 files** (BreadboardPartInspector 10/10, lifecycle-badges-integration 33/33, procurement-sub-components 30/30) with **0 ECONNREFUSED**. A11y command logged at `logs/tests-a11y-bl0876-after.log` still has known BL-0875 failures (**17 fail / 10 pass / 27 total**) but `ECONNREFUSED` is **0** vs **18** in `logs/tests-a11y-latest.log`; no regression requiring revert was observed. | DONE | C2 | BL-0866 split 2026-05-09 |

Verified: `npx vitest run client/src/components/circuit-editor/__tests__/BreadboardPartInspector.trustTier.test.tsx` → **10/10 passing in 14.9s**. | DONE | C1 | BL-0866 split 2026-05-09 |

Fix: extended existing `renderWithTooltip` helper to also wrap in `QueryClientProvider`. (b) `procurement-sub-components.test.tsx` 2 BomToolbar fails — same root cause.
