| BL-0879 | **CRDT conflict-detected message not emitted to LWW-losing client** — architectural fix CONVERGED via 8-round Claude↔Codex bidirectional adversarial review (2026-05-09). **READY FOR IMPLEMENTATION.** Converged spec consists of 3 documents: (1) `CODEX_PLAN_v2.md` — primary plan (sections 1-9: schema, server state, conflict detection, ACK semantics, state-sync gap, test plan, insert/delete carve, state-sync replay carve, LOC estimate); (2) `CLAUDE_RESPONSE_3.md` — adds BL-0883 cold-start carve regression test + `connectionId` server-only doc comment; (3) `CLAUDE_RESPONSE_5.md` — adds `markObserved` no-op-on-disconnect-race rule + `flushMicrotasks` primitive spec (`setImmediate`-backed) + same-frame duplicate test 5b. **Approach:** Option C-plus-ACK — per-connection ids on ClientEntry, server-owned per-key frontier `Map<projectId, Map<entityKey, FrontierEntry{ts, connectionId, userId, op}>>`, per-connection observedFrontier `Map<projectId, Map<connectionId, Map<entityKey, number>>>` advanced ONLY by client `state-update-ack` (reused for both normal remote updates AND conflict `theirOp` ACKs), conflict rule `frontier.ts > observedFrontier[C][K] && frontier.connectionId !== C`, theirOp stored in frontier for UI payload, server validates ACKs with `Math.max(current, ackedTs)` and `ackedTs ≤ frontier.ts` clamp. Carves: BL-0882 (insert/delete frontier extension), BL-0883 (state-sync document snapshot + replay + ACK), BL-0884 (per-connection ACK ledger for spoofing-proof CRDT integrity, future). LOC estimate **775-1305 changed lines** (380-620 production + 395-685 tests). **Pickup steps:** Fresh session reads the 3 spec docs in order, implements per CODEX_PLAN_v2.md §9 implementation order, runs 12 server integration tests + 5 client tests, then non-implementing agent (Codex if Claude implements; vice-versa) reviews the diff for final correctness. Bidirectional pattern continues into implementation phase. **DONE (verified 2026-07-10, BL-0908 reconciliation):** Full Option C-plus-ACK architecture implemented in code — `server/collaboration.ts:63-126,616,627,767; commit 26d08f86`. | DONE (BL-0908 reconciliation 2026-07-10) | C2 | Converged 2026-05-09 (8-round adversarial review) |

## P2 — Medium (Feature Gaps & Polish)


### Competitive Gaps — Core EDA (vs. KiCad/Altium/EasyEDA) (Omni-eval 2026-07-01)

| ID | Description | Status | Complexity | Source |
|----|-------------|--------|------------|--------|
| BL-0939 | **Engine PCB hard-wired to 2 copper layers, no stackup model** — `packages/export/src/gerber.ts:23` restricts export to `F.Cu|B.Cu` and vias are through-hole only (gerber.ts:104 'flash both copper layers'); rule decks are 2-layer-only (ROADMAP.md:2089-2091). The via `span` field already exists in the op schema (`packages/graph/src/ops.ts:198 zPlaceVia: span: z.tuple([...])`) but no exporter/DRC/renderer consumes it, and layerId is stored as a free string with no stackup entity. 4-layer boards (the standard ground-plane-pair pattern) are table stakes even at hobbyist fabs; this blocks any KiCad-refugee MCU design with a proper ground/power plane pair. | OPEN | C5 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: competitive-core-eda) |
| BL-0940 | **No KiCad/Eagle/EasyEDA import path in the engine** — `packages/cli/src/index.ts:119` ships only `import-legacy` (ProtoPulse's own Postgres-dump importer); `rg` for `kicad_sym|kicad_mod|eagle|easyeda` across `packages/` returns zero hits. The engine's seed library is 62 parts (`packages/parts/src/seed/index.ts`, 62 `core:` ids) vs. competitors' hundreds of thousands. No tool exists to turn `.kicad_sym`/`.kicad_mod` (where the vast majority of existing symbols/footprints live) into a `pp-part-pack`, so nobody with an existing design or library history can migrate onto the engine. | OPEN | C4 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: competitive-core-eda) |
| BL-0941 | **Hierarchical sheets/buses ops landed but have zero engine UI** — ROADMAP.md:96-101 confirms buses+sheets ops (create_bus/assign_to_bus/add_sheet/set_sheet_interface/move_to_sheet + inverses, GC, cycle detection) shipped 2026-06-11 with 'UI surfaces are a later slice'; `rg 'sheet'` in `packages/app/src` matches only `panels/ArchitectPanel.tsx` and `review/concepts.ts` — no sheet navigator, no sheet symbol on canvas, no enter/leave navigation, no bus tool. The legacy app already shipped an equivalent multi-sheet UI as BL-0104 (DONE, Wave 63 — SchematicSheetNode + Enter Sheet nav, a different surface — legacy client, not the engine), so this is a proven, well-understood UI pattern; the hard graph-algebra work is already done and tested in the engine, this is assembly-only. Cross-reference: BL-0104. | OPEN | C4 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: competitive-core-eda) |
| BL-0942 | **No 3D board viewer or STEP export anywhere in the engine** — `rg 'three|3d'` over `packages/app/src` and `packages/renderer/src` finds no 3D rendering code, and `Footprint` (`packages/parts/src/types.ts`) has no 3D-model binding field. `docs/vision/volume-1-ground-up-redesign.md:172` commits to '3D board view (three.js) with STEP export for enclosure CAD'; `volume-3-content-and-day-one.md:149` makes the Probe enclosure's board outline parametrically dependent on STEP export — yet the engine roadmap has zero corresponding entry. Even the legacy fallback doesn't deliver: `docs/FEATURE_MATURITY.md:47` confirms legacy `viewer_3d` is 'No actual 3D WebGL rendering (scene graph is data-only).' Related but distinct from BL-0887 (legacy `client/src/components/views/BoardViewer3DView.tsx` photoreal-rendering upgrade, in-progress in `worktree-3d-viewer`) — that work is scoped entirely to the legacy client and does not touch STEP export or the engine's `packages/` surface. Cross-reference: BL-0887. | OPEN | C5 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: competitive-core-eda) |
| BL-0943 | **No custom-part authoring tooling in the engine, including the cheap fallback** — the 22-panel app has no library/symbol/footprint editor, and parts enter only via `SEED_PARTS` or file-loaded packs (ROADMAP.md:2071-2080). Worse, the fallback path (hand-authoring `pp-part-pack` JSON) is unsupported too: `packages/parts/src/seed/helpers.ts` already has the exact helper functions that would make it tractable (`definePart`, `footprint0805`, `footprintDip8`, `footprintSot23`, `twoPinSymbol`, `passivePins`) but `packages/parts/src/index.ts:5-13` exports only `types`, `PartDb`/`seedPartDb`, `SEED_PARTS`, and the pack.ts symbols — never the helpers. No README, no example pack file, no CLI validate command exists in `packages/parts/`. | OPEN | C5 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: competitive-core-eda) |
| BL-0944 | **No differential-pair routing or length/skew tuning in `@protopulse/route`** — `packages/route/src/index.ts` exports only `obstacles`/`walkaround`/`shove`/`pour`; there is no pair-aware routing entity. KiCad 8 ships Route Single Track, Route Differential Pair, Tune Length, and Tune Diff-Pair Skew with meander patterns. Legacy already built `diff-pair-router.ts` + `diff-pair-meander.ts` (BL-0500/BL-0510, DONE — legacy-only, a different surface than the engine), a portable reference implementation; nets already carry `netClass` (`zSetNetClass` in ops.ts) so pair identification has a substrate. USB, RGMII, and LVDS boards all need this — it's typically the first thing a KiCad user checks when evaluating a router. Cross-reference: BL-0510. | OPEN | C4 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: competitive-core-eda) |
| BL-0945 | **Engine DRC deck covers only 9 of the vision-specified rule codes** — `packages/drc/src/codes.ts:8-16` lists the complete implemented set (TRACE-WIDTH, CLEARANCE, ANNULAR, DRILL, UNROUTED, ZONE-OVERLAP, ZONE-ISOLATED, EDGE-CLEARANCE, UNSUPPORTED-ROTATION) — no courtyard-overlap, hole-to-hole, silk-over-pad, paste-sanity, drill-to-copper, acid-trap, starved-thermal, or zone-sliver checks, despite `docs/vision/volume-2-deep-spec.md` §C.2 specifying a substantially larger deck. `Footprint.courtyard` (`packages/parts/src/types.ts:75`) already carries `{wNm, hNm}` data that no rule consumes — courtyard-overlap is checkable-but-unimplemented, i.e. this gap is 'ready, not blocked.' Distinct from BL-0507 (legacy DRC's solder-mask/paste/assembly rules, DONE Wave 72, a different surface) — that work never touched the engine's `packages/drc`. Cross-reference: BL-0507. | OPEN | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: competitive-core-eda) |
| BL-0946 | **No PDF/SVG plot of schematic or PCB in the engine** — `packages/export/src` contains `bom-csv, excellon, gerber, kicad-netlist, panel, pick-place, sexpr` and no pdf/svg/plot module; the Export panel offers no print path. Legacy has `server/export/pdf-generator.ts` + `pdf-report-generator.ts`, and every mainstream EDA tool (KiCad included) prints hyperlinked, searchable-text schematic/fab drawings. The renderer already lays out everything in integer-nm space, so an SVG emitter (with print-to-PDF as the browser's native path) is a comparatively cheap win that immediately unlocks design review and off-tool documentation. | OPEN | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: competitive-core-eda) |
| BL-0947 | **No vendor SPICE model (.subckt/.lib) attach path for parts** — `packages/sim/src/models.ts` compiles in built-in emitters with a declared `ModelTier = 'spice' | 'behavioral' | 'stub'` per part, but no op, part field, or UI accepts a user-supplied model file — every non-seed part is permanently stuck at `stub` tier. KiCad's simulator lets any symbol reference an external SPICE model file, which is how real op-amps/regulators get simulated from manufacturer models. A `.subckt` attach flow (pack field + netlist include) would convert the fidelity-bar honesty system from a hard ceiling into a usable ladder. | OPEN | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: competitive-core-eda) |
| BL-0948 | **No batch 'route all' — every connection is routed one at a time** — `packages/route` exports only per-connection primitives (`walkaround`/`shove`); the Router AI crew member drives single connections via `route_connection` tool calls (ROADMAP.md:90-95), and there is no ratsnest-ordered batch orchestrator. DRC-UNROUTED (`codes.ts:12`) reports what's missing but nothing offers to finish it. An 80%-good deterministic batch pass (net-ordered, walk-first/shove-when-cornered, one undoable batch) is achievable on maker-class 2-layer boards without new geometry code, and would also make the Router agent dramatically cheaper by replacing one LLM round-trip per net. | OPEN | C4 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: competitive-core-eda) |
| BL-0949 | **Footprint rotation pinned to 90° steps in the engine** — `packages/export/src/pcb-common.ts:9-11` documents 'Only 90° steps are supported; other angles snap to the nearest quarter turn (the pinned op contract only emits 0/90/180/270),' and `packages/drc/src/codes.ts:16` / `run.ts:174` dedicate a `DRC-UNSUPPORTED-ROTATION` code to flagging it. KiCad places footprints at arbitrary angles (0.1° resolution), routine for radial layouts and connectors. `rotMilli` already stores millidegrees, so the data model is done — the debt is confined to placement transform, DRC hulls, and the Gerber/pick-and-place emit paths. This gap also silently caps finding on KiCad import: any real-world imported design with non-90° placements would degrade on import. | OPEN | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: competitive-core-eda) |
| BL-0950 | **No on-canvas net labels or name autocomplete while wiring in the engine** — `packages/app/src/panels/Inspector.tsx:76` (`rename_net`) is the only net-naming path; no net-label entity renders on canvas, and while power symbols exist (`core:pwr-gnd`/`core:pwr-vcc` in `packages/parts/src/seed/index.ts`) named local/global labels don't. Without labels every connection must be physically wired on canvas, which stops scaling around 20 components — the same gap logged against the legacy app as BL-0798 (OPEN, 'KiCad shows VCC/GND/NET-NAME autocomplete', a different surface — legacy client) but never closed there either, and the engine has no equivalent surface at all yet. Cross-reference: BL-0798. | OPEN | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: competitive-core-eda) |
| BL-0951 | **No cross-probing between schematic and PCB views in the engine editor** — `rg 'crossprobe|cross-probe|highlightNet'` across `packages/app/src` returns zero hits; Schematic/PCB is a plain mode toggle with no selection sync. KiCad's default cross-probing (click a part/net in one editor, it highlights in the other) is relied on constantly during placement and routing. Because both engine views materialize from the same graph, this is likely the cheapest high-visibility parity win available — selection sync just needs to share selected entity IDs across scenes. | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: competitive-core-eda) |

### Competitive Gaps — Web/Maker Tools (vs. Wokwi/Flux/Falstad/Tinkercad) (Omni-eval 2026-07-01)

| ID | Description | Status | Complexity | Source |
|----|-------------|--------|------------|--------|
| BL-0952 | **Firmware panel requires a pre-compiled .hex/.bin — no compile-from-source in the engine** — `packages/app/src/panels/FirmwarePanel.tsx:164` placeholder literally reads '…paste a compiled .hex here, or pick a file below,' and `rg 'compile|arduino-cli|toolchain'` over the panel finds nothing. The legacy app already ships an arduino-cli sidecar (`ARDUINO_CLI_PATH`/`ARDUINO_SKETCH_ROOT` env, per `.ref/project-dna.md:84`) that the engine app never wires in. Wokwi and Tinkercad both accept source and build it for you; requiring an external toolchain undercuts the 'zero-install browser lab' promise that's this category's core selling point. | OPEN | C4 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: competitive-web-maker) |
| BL-0953 | **No interactive bench inputs during emulation** — the McuCore contract already supports external pin drive and cycle-stamped pin events (ROADMAP.md ~225-228), but the only runtime input surface is the serial monitor in `FirmwarePanel.tsx`; there are no virtual button/switch/pot widgets, and `CosimPanel` only runs batch closed-loop quanta (`packages/cosim/src/quantum.ts`). Wokwi, Tinkercad, EveryCircuit, and Crumb all center their UX on poking live components mid-simulation. The substrate (pin-drive + ADC sampler) already exists — a bench-widget strip bound to a pin/ADC channel is mostly UI work and would make the Firmware/Cosim panels demoable head-to-head against Wokwi. | OPEN | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: competitive-web-maker) |
| BL-0954 | **No firmware debugger surface — no breakpoints, single-step, register inspection, or GDB remote protocol** — `rg -i 'breakpoint|gdb'` over `packages/emu/src` returns no matches (the nearest related text is a passing 'debuggers expect' comment at `xtensa.ts:17`); `FirmwarePanel` offers only run/pause plus logic-analyzer traces, and no VCD export exists anywhere in `packages/app`/`packages/emu`. Wokwi advertises GDB debugging (breakpoints + variable inspection) as a headline feature. Because the fetch/decode loop is hand-written, PC-match breakpoints, single-step, and register dumps are comparatively cheap to add, and a GDB-RSP stub over WebSocket would let real toolchains (VS Code cortex-debug, xtensa-gdb) attach to the browser emulator directly. | OPEN | C4 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: competitive-web-maker) |
| BL-0955 | **CLI has no headless firmware-test command** — `packages/cli/src/index.ts` implements only `check`, `export`, and `import-legacy` (confirmed at lines 38/92/119); meanwhile the repo's own test suite already runs firmware deterministically against emulated cores with serial/pin assertions and closed-loop SPICE co-sim (`packages/cosim/src/quantum.esp32s3.cosim.test.ts`). Wokwi sells exactly this capability as its paid 'CI/CD' tier. A `protopulse run-firmware <image> --core esp32s3 --expect-serial 'OK' --max-cycles N [--cosim spec.json]` command is a thin wrapper over existing library code and would productize the emulation campaign's 1,600+ tests worth of infrastructure as a user-facing feature — closed-loop analog co-sim in CI is something Wokwi can't do at all. | OPEN | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: competitive-web-maker) |
| BL-0956 | **Curriculum Track UI is unbuilt — content pipeline exists, zero consuming panel** — `content/tracks/01-first-light` ships machine-checkable goal steps (`packages/content/src/index.ts:3`), but no Track/Learn panel exists in `packages/app/src/panels/` (verified full listing — ConceptViewer/PuzzlePanel exist, no Track surface) and `rg -i track` over the panel dir turns up only unrelated string matches ("leaves tracks", a stale comment in `DraftsmanPanel.tsx:3`). The vision's education pillar (Tracks 1-7) has an executable-goal differentiator over Tinkercad's static lessons but nothing renders it. A Track panel that loads steps, checks the goal against the live graph, and hands off to the Professor on failure would ship the pillar. | OPEN | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: competitive-web-maker) |
| BL-0957 | **Engine app opens on an empty design with no example/template gallery** — `packages/app/src/state/session.ts:255` boots `new SessionCore(newUuid())` unconditionally when no bundle is passed; `rg -i 'example|template|starter|demo|fixture'` over `App.tsx`/`session.ts` returns nothing. Test-fixture `.ppx` designs (555 astable, RC regulator, cosim demos) already exist in the repo but aren't loadable from the UI. An "Open example" gallery seeded from owned fixtures is low-cost and doubles as living documentation of sim/emu/cosim features. | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: competitive-web-maker) |
| BL-0958 | **Sync relay has ops convergence but zero presence/awareness** — `rg -i 'presence|cursor|avatar'` over `packages/relay/src` and `packages/app/src/panels/SyncPanel.tsx` returns zero matches; the relay unions actor/lamport op envelopes only. Legacy already shipped WebSocket-room presence UX (BL-0549, DONE Wave 106) but the engine's CRDT-based relay has no equivalent — live op sync currently feels "haunted" (wires appear with no indication of who). An ephemeral presence side-channel (actor/color/viewport/selection, never entering the op-log) is the natural next slice after CRDT sync. Cross-reference: BL-0549. | OPEN | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: competitive-web-maker) |
| BL-0959 | **No live/interactive simulation mode in the engine — batch-only sim vs. Falstad/EveryCircuit's always-running animated current** — Engine sim runs op/tran/dc/ac decks to completion with a post-run ghost overlay tinting wires by solved voltage (`ROADMAP.md` v0.2 sim section); `rg -i 'live|interactive'` over `SimPanel.tsx` returns nothing, and no parameter is tweakable mid-run. Legacy shipped an equivalent (BL-0128, live current/voltage animation overlay, DONE Wave 77) but it's legacy-only — the engine has no analogue. A tractable slice: loop short tran windows continuously, animate current dots from branch currents onto the existing ghost-overlay pipeline, and re-emit the deck on live value edits. Cross-reference: BL-0128. | OPEN | C4 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: competitive-web-maker) |
| BL-0960 | **Six separate AI crew panels, no unified "ask anything" front door** — `packages/app/src/panels/` ships DraftsmanPanel, AnalystPanel, ProfessorPanel, RouterPanel, ArchitectPanel, BuyerPanel as six independent tabs (confirmed via `App.tsx` panel imports); `packages/ai/src/router.ts`'s "Router" is the PCB-routing agent (read_board/route_connection/run_drc tools), not an intent dispatcher, and `rg 'classif|intent' packages/ai/src` returns nothing. A beginner has no way to know which specialist "why is my LED dim" belongs to. One Crew chat that classifies intent and dispatches to the existing scoped tool registries turns six good specialists into one great copilot without touching the runtime. | OPEN | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: competitive-web-maker) |
| BL-0961 | **Graph.diff()/Review.diffReports() are fully built but unwired to any "request review" surface** — `packages/graph/src/diff.ts:118` exports `diff(a, b): GraphDelta`; `packages/review/src/diff.ts` exports `diffReports(a, b): ReviewDiff` (opened/closed finding deltas, confirmed via `review.test.ts` and `index.ts` re-export) — neither is wired to a CLI command or PR-comment surface (`packages/cli/src/index.ts` registers only `check`/`export`/`import-legacy`). Branches-as-pointers, three-way merge, and diffable review reports are all shipped; nothing productizes them into a "request review" flow — the clearest unshipped differentiator vs. GitHub-less competitors. | OPEN | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: competitive-web-maker) |
| BL-0962 | **Device models are core-blessed only — part packs can't ship a simulatable I2C/SPI model** — All four modeled devices (MPU-6050, BME280, DS3231, RC522) are hardcoded behind `I2C_DEVICES_BY_PART_ID`/`SPI_DEVICES_BY_PART_ID` registries in `packages/emu/src/i2c-devices.ts` / `spi-devices.ts` (confirmed; BL-0892/0893/0895/0896/0897/0898/0899/0900, all DONE); the `pp-part-pack` format carries only Part records, no device-model payload, and none of those items' "remaining/optional" follow-ups mention a pack-extensible model. Extending packs with a declarative register-map (or sandboxed JS module implementing the existing `I2cSlave`/`SpiSlave` interface) converts the co-sim device layer from a 4-device demo into a community-extensible platform — the provenance-tier vocabulary already exists to gate trust. | OPEN | C4 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: competitive-web-maker) |
| BL-0963 | **Engine editor has no touch/pinch support — pointer+wheel only** — `packages/app/src/editor/CanvasHost.tsx` wires `pointerdown/move/up/leave` + `wheel` only (confirmed at the cited handlers); `rg -i 'pinch|touches|gesture'` across the file and `packages/app/src` returns zero relevant matches (only unrelated `opTouches`/"user touches the checkboxes" hits). Legacy already shipped this (BL-0345, gesture shortcuts pinch-zoom/two-finger-pan, DONE Wave 113, `client/src/lib/gesture-shortcuts.ts`) but it's legacy-app-only — the engine app is desktop-only. Pointer events are already the input layer; tracking active pointers + pinch-zoom/two-finger-pan + enlarged hit tolerances is incremental. Cross-reference: BL-0345. | OPEN | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: competitive-web-maker) |
| BL-0964 | **No 3D board view in the engine app** — `packages/app/src/panels/` has no 3D panel (confirmed full listing); the legacy `viewer_3d` "has no actual 3D WebGL rendering (scene graph is data-only)" (`docs/FEATURE_MATURITY.md:47`, confirmed verbatim), and the photoreal FR4 rebuild (tracked informally as "BL-0887" only in session memory/worktree notes — not a formalized `MASTER_BACKLOG.md` row) is uncommitted Phase-1 work targeting legacy `client/`. The engine already has all geometry (footprints, traces, zones, board thickness) in integer-nm; extruding it in the existing WebGL2 renderer is bounded and should target the engine graph rather than the legacy-only viewer. | OPEN | C4 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: competitive-web-maker) |
| BL-0965 | **Breadboard has no engine projection and no ROADMAP milestone** — `packages/graph/src/index.ts` header promises "One graph, many projections: schematic, breadboard, PCB, BOM" but `rg -i breadboard ROADMAP.md` returns zero engine-milestone matches and `packages/app` has no breadboard panel (confirmed). Legacy's Breadboard Lab absorbed a 399-finding audit investment and is the maker entry-point competitors (Tinkercad/Fritzing/Crumb) use to onboard beginners. When the migration milestone flips the default UI to the engine app, this workflow silently disappears unless it gets a milestone now — deciding the engine breadboard projection (same op-log, emu bench attached) prevents rebuilding legacy's mistakes. | OPEN | C5 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: competitive-web-maker) |
| BL-0966 | **Time-lapse replay has no exportable/shareable artifact** — Replay ships as an editor-internal History-tab scrubber plus a developer-only GIF capture script (`tools/screenshots/capture-replay-gif.ts`, confirmed); `rg replay` across `packages/app/src` finds no share-link or `?replay=` auto-scrub mechanism (only the internal `replay.ts`/`replay.test.ts` state module). BL-0144 (hardware session recorder, DONE) is a different feature — Arduino serial/action logs, not design-history replay. A "Share replay" button (share link + `?replay=1` auto-scrub, or a one-click GIF/WebM export reusing the existing capture path) turns the op-log's core thesis into marketing no competitor (Falstad/CircuitLab share static circuits) can copy. | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: competitive-web-maker) |
| BL-0967 | **Buyer agent reasons over a static 9-part catalog — no live "is it in stock" answer** — `content/catalog/jlc-assembly-seed.json` is a rev-stamped snapshot of 9 hand-verified LCSC numbers, explicitly "no prices or stock BY DESIGN" (`ROADMAP.md` v0.3 Buyer section, confirmed verbatim); live vendor APIs are deferred to the v0.6+ manufacturing pipeline, an open "needs Tyler" checkbox. The honesty stance is right but concedes the whole sourcing workflow to competitors (Flux's 750K-part live-pricing library). A read-only live-offers tool behind the Buyer (Mouser/Digi-Key public search APIs, response timestamped "live quote, fetched now") preserves the honesty principle while removing the gap — scoped to the agent tool layer, not the deferred ordering pipeline. | OPEN | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: competitive-web-maker) |
| BL-0968 | **No inbound design-import path except ProtoPulse's own legacy DB dump** — `packages/cli/src/index.ts:119` registers `import-legacy` (legacy Postgres snapshot → `.ppx`) as the sole importer; confirmed no KiCad netlist, Falstad text-format, or Fritzing import exists anywhere in `packages/` (KiCad references are export-only, `packages/export/src/kicad-netlist.ts`). Every competitor's user carries existing designs; a Falstad text-format importer is a weekend-scale win (plain-text, thousands of public circuits become instant examples for the gallery gap in BL-0957), and KiCad netlist import can reuse the exporter's own mapping tables in reverse. | OPEN | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: competitive-web-maker) |

| ID | Description | Status | Complexity | Source |
|----|-------------|--------|------------|--------|
| BL-0969 | **WebSerial/WebUSB flashing is the last non-opportunistic v0.5 checkbox, blocked on hardware since ~2026-06-11, and is now unblocked.** `ROADMAP.md:242` `- [ ] WebSerial/WebUSB flashing (needs real hardware to verify)` is the only open v0.5 item besides the explicitly non-gating ESP32 long tail (`ROADMAP.md:2033-2037`). The bench now has real ESP32/AVR hardware (ESP32-S3 completion gate E2E certified 2026-06-23), and legacy already ships a working `WebSerialManager` + esptool/avrdude parsing (BL-0147, BL-0482/0483, all DONE) as a port reference. Closing this completes v0.5 'The Bridge' — a full vision stage, and the first physical-bridge feature — per Vol I §7.1 (`volume-1:183`): 'the export isn't a zip file; it's electrons in the chip.' | OPEN | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: vision-drift) |
| BL-0970 | **Migration criterion 2 (run Tyler's real projects through the legacy→.ppx importer) has been sitting unblocked since 2026-06-11 and gates the default-UI flip.** `ROADMAP.md:2113-2125`: the importer landed 2026-06-11 (`protopulse import-legacy`), materialize-verified and `protopulse check`-clean, with 'Still open: moving Tyler's REAL projects through it (criterion 2 below needs his database)' — the database is local to the same box (`server/db.ts` `DATABASE_URL`). All downstream migration items (default-UI flip, area-by-area retirement) wait on it, with no recorded attempt in twenty days. One SQL dump + one CLI run per project produces either migrated designs or a concrete importer bug list — the cheapest way to make the whole migration milestone real. | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: vision-drift) |
| BL-0971 | **The learning layer is 1 of 7 tracks and 1 of 12 puzzles built — tracks 2-6, the failure-puzzle catalog, and the skill map are absent from the roadmap entirely.** Vision Vol III §1.2 specs seven tracks and §1.4 a 12-puzzle seed catalog; §1.3 the skill-map DAG (`volume-3:32-65`). On disk: `content/tracks/` has only `01-first-light` (5 steps, verified `ls`), `content/puzzles/` has only `slow-rise-11`. `rg -in 'track [0-9]|puzzle|skill map' ROADMAP.md` finds no entry for tracks 2-6, the puzzle catalog, or the skill map (only Track 1 at line 20 and Track 7 as the v0.7 capstone at line 2135). Legacy tutor items (BL-0300/0301/0460) are a different surface. Design thesis #3 ('the simulator is the teacher') is the market differentiator vs Wokwi/KiCad — the engine now has every substrate the tracks need (sim, co-sim, ERC assertions as goal checks), the content is unblocked, but the vision's most user-facing promise sits at ~8-14% completion with nothing scheduled. | OPEN | C4 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: vision-drift) |
| BL-0972 | **Six of seven graph constraint kinds are defined but never consumed** — `packages/graph/src/types.ts:97-104` defines `ConstraintBody` kinds `current_max`/`voltage_domain`/`diff_pair`/`length_match`/`keepout`/`thermal_limit`/`clearance_override`; only `current_max` is ever read (`packages/erc/src/run.ts:276`). No rule in `packages/erc`, `packages/drc`, `packages/route`, or `packages/review` branches on the other six kinds, and ROADMAP.md has no diff-pair/length-match/keepout DRC or routing entry. The op-log layer (`packages/graph/src/ops.ts` `zConstraintBody`) accepts all seven — Draftsman's `add_constraint` AI tool is deliberately restricted to `current_max` only, but any code constructing ops directly can create the other six with zero downstream effect. | OPEN | C4 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: vision-drift) |

Claude Code drives ProtoPulse headlessly"), `ROADMAP.md` has zero MCP entries, and no backlog item covers it. The substrate is largely ready: `packages/ai/src/registry.ts:26-33` already has a typed `ToolRegistry` with zod schemas, `destructive` gating, and `CostClass` tags, and `@protopulse/cli` proves headless operation — wrapping the existing registry in an MCP transport is a much smaller lift than building the tool surface from scratch. | OPEN | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: vision-drift) |

Legacy already has a working arduino-cli sidecar (ADR-0007, `ARDUINO_CLI_PATH`) that is never scheduled for porting, making this a port rather than a research project. | OPEN | C4 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: vision-drift) |

No ADR in `docs/adr/` covers sync, despite the deviation likely being the correct call — it just needs the paper trail with a "Revisit when" clause. | OPEN | C1 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: vision-drift) |
| BL-0977 | **`docs/FEATURE_MATURITY.md` Table 4 is stale in both directions despite claiming a 2026-06-16 refresh** — line 201 says "no interactive resolver UI yet" while `ROADMAP.md:25-29` and `docs/adr/README.md:23` confirm the resolver landed 2026-06-10; line 195 cites "346 tests" against ~1,626 live engine cases (`.ref/project-dna.md:12`); Table 1 covers 30 ViewModes vs. 36 defined in `client/src/lib/project-context.tsx:139` (six legacy views never classified: `supply_chain`, `bom_templates`, `personal_inventory`, `part_alternates`, `part_usage`, `vault_browser`). This is the project's designated honesty ledger per AGENTS.md — its own drift corrupts every downstream migration/maturity decision that reads it. | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: vision-drift) |

### EDA Domain Correctness (Omni-eval 2026-07-01)

| ID | Description | Status | Complexity | Source |
|----|-------------|--------|------------|--------|
| BL-0978 | **Engine DRC has no via aspect-ratio rule, and the deck schema has no board-thickness field to support one** — `packages/content/src/schemas.ts`'s `deckRulesSchema` (lines 12-19) has 6 fields, none for board thickness or aspect ratio; `ruleViaGeometry` in `packages/drc/src/run.ts` checks drill and annular ring only. Legacy already solved this exact problem (`client/src/lib/pcb/pcb-drc-checker.ts` `via_n_ratio` check, tracked as BL-0508, DONE) — aspect ratio is one of the most common real-world reasons a Gerber set gets bounced at fab, independent of annular ring being fine, so the engine loses a real DFM guardrail on migration. Cross-reference: BL-0508. | OPEN | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: eda-domain) |
| BL-0979 | **Engine BOM CSV export drops Manufacturer entirely and has no Package/footprint-name field to include even if it tried** — `packages/export/src/bom-csv.ts`'s header is `Refs,Qty,Value,Part,MPN,DNP` (line 47); the `Group` interface (16-22) never reads `part.manufacturer` despite `Part.manufacturer` existing (`packages/parts/src/types.ts:102`), and `Part` has no descriptive package-name string field at all. Legacy's `server/export/bom-exporter.ts` supports Manufacturer/MPN columns across vendor exports (Digi-Key/Mouser/JLC, lines 150-456) plus a package-name extractor — assembly-ready BOM formats (JLC SMT, PCBWay assembly) require both fields to auto-match line items, so this is a functional regression on the engine export meant to eventually replace the legacy one. | OPEN | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: eda-domain) |
| BL-0980 | **Excellon exporter joins plated and non-plated holes into one drill file — a self-documented gap that panelization mouse-bites already ride** — `packages/export/src/excellon.ts:14-17`: "Honest note: they join this one drill file — KiCad would split plated/non-plated into separate PTH/NPTH files, we don't yet." Harmless today since `FootprintPad` has no plated/unplated flag, but `ExtraHole` (panelization mouse-bite perforations, conventionally non-plated) already merges into the same file via the `type:2` hit path (lines 26-29, 70-79) — once panelization ships mouse-bites, a fab CAM front-end that infers plating from which Excellon file a hole appears in could plate holes meant to snap cleanly. | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: eda-domain) |

Professor's `explain_finding` tool (`packages/ai/src/tools/professor.ts:94-136`) only maps a finding code the caller already has to a concept article — no persona can run a review pass itself or act on what it finds. | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: ai-crew-gaps) |
| BL-0984 | **No test-point/testbench-planning tool in the engine crew — legacy's `suggest_testbench`/`explain_test_point`/`generate_test_sequence` has zero engine parity** — `server/ai-tools/testbench.ts` (578 lines) implements topology-based test recommendations and step-by-step verification procedures (BL-0163, DONE Wave 87); `packages/ai/src/tools/analyst.ts`'s 3 tools (`run_simulation`, `measure`, `read_design`) never suggest what to simulate — the model must already know. A well-scoped, self-contained port target with proven maker value, not a novel design problem. Cross-reference: BL-0163. | OPEN | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: ai-crew-gaps) |
| BL-0985 | **Buyer has no cost-optimization or alternate-part-suggestion tool — legacy's BOM optimization has zero engine parity** — `server/ai-tools/bom-optimization.ts` registers `analyze_bom_optimization`/`suggest_alternate_part`/`consolidate_packages` with real package-size/cost-tier tables (BL-0164, DONE Wave 88); `packages/ai/src/tools/buyer.ts`'s 4 tools can only assign an LCSC# the caller already identified — no tool searches for a cheaper equivalent or flags over-spec'd parts. Package-size consolidation and over-spec detection don't require live pricing (the catalog is deliberately price-free per `ROADMAP.md:109-113`), so this is structural BOM analysis the current data already supports. Cross-reference: BL-0164. | OPEN | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: ai-crew-gaps) |
| BL-0986 | **`AnthropicProvider` is fully non-streaming — every crew turn blocks until the whole model response completes** — `packages/ai/src/anthropic.ts:32-38` calls `messages.create` with no `stream: true`; `packages/ai/src/provider.ts:4` documents this as a deliberate M1 decision ("Non-streaming is fine for M1"). Legacy ships real per-token SSE streaming with heartbeats (`server/routes/chat.ts:543-547`, BL-0288 DONE). A documented descope, not an oversight, but now concretely measurable against a shipped baseline: an Analyst/Router loop with a heavy `run_simulation` call shows nothing until each multi-second turn completes. | OPEN | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: ai-crew-gaps) |
| BL-0987 | **`CostClass` tags exist on every AI tool but nothing in the runtime consumes them for model routing** — `packages/ai/src/registry.ts:26` defines `CostClass = 'cheap' | 'standard' | 'heavy'` and every tool is tagged per `packages/ai/README.md`'s Cost Classes section ("so a dispatcher/UI can reason about per-call expense"), but `packages/ai/src/anthropic.ts:27-28` pins one model per `AnthropicProvider` instance for the whole persona run — no code path selects a cheaper tier for cheap/read-only calls (e.g. Buyer's `find_offers`) versus a heavier one for complex reasoning. The metadata already exists; only the dispatcher logic that reads it is missing. | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: ai-crew-gaps) |
| BL-0988 | **Analyst's SPICE analysis schema has no thermal, noise, or Monte Carlo tolerance kind** — `packages/ai/src/tools/analyst.ts:22-48`'s `zAnalysis` union covers exactly `op`/`tran`/`dc`/`ac`. The legacy simulation surface (MASTER_BACKLOG.md:1485) explicitly lists thermal analysis and Monte Carlo tolerance stack-up as shipped capabilities, and BL-0502 (DONE) already renders thermal heatmaps client-side from `thermal-analysis.ts` — but that's a legacy PCB-view overlay, not a SPICE analysis kind the engine's Analyst persona can run or reason about. The Analyst's whole pitch is 'skeptical until it's plotted,' yet it structurally cannot answer 'will this part run hot' or 'what's my worst-case tolerance stack-up.' False-flagged as a BL-0502 duplicate — different surface (engine AI tool schema vs. legacy PCB overlay); cross-reference only. Cross-reference: BL-0502. | OPEN | C4 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: ai-crew-gaps) |
| BL-0989 | **No image/vision content block anywhere in the engine AI crew** — `packages/ai/src/provider.ts:11-14`'s `ProviderContentBlock` union is `text`/`tool_use`/`tool_result` only; `packages/ai/src/anthropic.ts` never constructs an image block when calling the Messages API, despite Claude's own Messages API supporting multimodal input natively. Legacy already ships the analog: `server/ai-tools/vision.ts`'s `identify_component_from_image` takes a base64 photo and returns component type/package/MPN/pin-count/confidence plus suggested BOM fields. The gap is purely that `ProviderContentBlock`/`LlmTurn` were never extended to carry images — not a model capability limitation. | OPEN | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: ai-crew-gaps) |
| BL-0990 | **No AI-crew tool queries the feature-complete @protopulse/emu emulator** — `rg "@protopulse/emu" packages/ai/src packages/ai/evals` returns zero matches. `packages/emu` (AVR/RP2040/ESP32-S3) reached feature-complete and was gate-certified 2026-06-23, and legacy's Pre-flight Check already validates categories like 'ESP32 ADC2/WiFi Conflict' and 'Required Pin Connections' (BL-0767, OPEN — it falsely passes on an empty board, but the check categories are product-validated). No Draftsman/Analyst/Architect tool wires into the emulator to authoritatively answer strapping-pin conflicts, ADC-mux sharing, or peripheral-availability questions — the hard engineering (the emulator) is done; only the AI-tool wiring is missing, making this the highest-leverage AI-crew gap on the list. Cross-reference: BL-0767. | OPEN | C4 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: ai-crew-gaps) |
| BL-0991 | **No firmware codegen/compile/upload tool in the engine AI crew** — `server/ai-tools/arduino.ts` registers `generate_arduino_sketch`, `compile_sketch`, `upload_firmware`, `search_arduino_libraries`, `list_arduino_boards` wired through the arduino-cli sidecar; `packages/ai/src` and `packages/cli/src` have zero references to arduino-cli, sketch generation, or firmware output. Combined with the emu-bridge gap (BL-0990), this leaves 'schematic to working prototype' entirely to the legacy app — a natural two-part roadmap item: generate a pin-mux-correct firmware scaffold, then validate it in @protopulse/emu before real hardware. | OPEN | C4 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: ai-crew-gaps) |
| BL-0992 | **Draftsman's `add_constraint` tool hardcodes 1 of 6 graph-supported constraint kinds** — `packages/graph/src/ops.ts`'s `add_constraint` body union defines `current_max`/`voltage_domain`/`diff_pair`/`length_match`/`keepout`/`thermal_limit`/`clearance_override` (verified: all present at lines 42-64), but `packages/ai/src/tools/draftsman.ts:339-345` hardcodes `kind: z.literal('current_max')` with the comment 'M1 supports only current_max.' `thermal_limit`/`keepout` map directly onto thermal-sanity and EMC asks; `voltage_domain`/`diff_pair`/`length_match` are constraints an Architect/Router needs declared before acting. Graph-level plumbing (schema, inverse ops, ERC hooks) already exists for all 6 — only the tool surface is narrow, making this a low-risk, high-value next slice. | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: ai-crew-gaps) |

| ID | Description | Status | Complexity | Source |

| BL-0993 | **ERC autofix CLI — apply `Finding.fix` op arrays instead of only printing them** — `packages/erc/src/findings.ts:19` defines `Finding.fix?: OpBody[]` and `packages/erc/src/index.ts:3` documents findings as carrying "executable fixes," but `packages/cli/src/index.ts`'s `check` action only reads findings for severity counts/`message` — `finding.fix` is never referenced anywhere in `packages/cli/src` (verified via grep). Add `protopulse autofix <design> --apply` (or `check --fix`) that applies each finding's op array through the existing graph op-apply machinery and writes back / opens a bot-authored commit — the ERC-linter-with-teeth other EDA tools (KiCad/Altium) don't have, since the data model already made fixes machine-executable. | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: moonshots) |
| BL-0994 | **Golden-file regression harness for SPICE/cosim waveforms, not just exports** — `tools/golden/` (per `tools/golden/README.md:1-5`) scopes golden testing to byte-exact op-log→export fixtures (KiCad/Gerber/BOM); no `tools/golden/fixtures/` entry covers transient sim or cosim traces. `packages/sim/src/montecarlo.ts:14-18` is already deterministic-by-seed and `packages/cosim` closed-loop tests already assert exact per-commit voltage/register values, so the two halves (golden-export convention + deterministic sim engine) exist independently but nobody connected them. Build a tolerance-banded golden-waveform harness (`protopulse check --regression`) that fails CI when a circuit/firmware change shifts a transient trace or cosim pin trace beyond threshold — the missing mechanism behind the vision doc's flagship "regulator now sags under load" pitch. | OPEN | C4 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: moonshots) |
| BL-0995 | **Professor-generated fault-injection puzzles to scale curriculum past 1 track** — `content/tracks/` contains only `01-first-light` and `content/puzzles/` contains only `slow-rise-11` (verified via `find`); `rg -n "generatePuzzle|puzzle.*generat" packages -i` returns zero matches — puzzles are entirely hand-authored, while `ROADMAP.md:2132-2137` promises a v0.7 "Track 7 capstone" with no content pipeline in between. Since `PuzzlePanel.tsx` already treats solved-ness as an op-log property and every op is invertible, a Professor-persona tool could apply a plausible fault op (swapped net, wrong value, missing pull-up — patterns already in ERC's fix-op vocabulary) to a golden fixture and auto-generate a new puzzle with the inverse op as verified ground truth — directly attacking the content-authoring bottleneck standing between today's 1-track state and the v0.7 promise. | OPEN | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: moonshots) |

A few files import each other in a self-contained dead subgraph (e.g. `push-shove-preview.ts` → `push-shove-engine.ts` → `PushShovePreviewRenderer.tsx`, itself never rendered) rather than being fully isolated, but no path reaches the live UI. `@protopulse/route` is the engine's future router — this 9.6k-line subsystem plus its tests should be deleted, not migrated, and the decision should be made explicit. | OPEN | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: code-health-legacy) |

A resistor edited in one panel can simulate or export as a subtly different value. Consolidating legacy call sites onto `@protopulse/graph`'s value-parser is a low-risk first migration step that also removes the CCN-44 `parseSINumber` hotspot. | OPEN | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: code-health-legacy) |

A comment in `drc-engine.ts` claims parity is enforced via `shared/__tests__/cross-engine-parity.test.ts`, but that file does not exist in the repo (only `units.test.ts`'s internal "milToMeter/meterToMil — full cross-engine chain" describe block covers legacy-internal parity) — nothing asserts legacy-mil DRC and engine-nm DRC agree on the same design. An ADR plus a small mil→nm parity fixture prevents silent clearance-check regressions when DRC migrates. | OPEN | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: code-health-legacy) |

No BL item covers it. Unlike frozen legacy files, this one keeps changing as new board-audit rules land; split each check into a rule module behind a shared registry so new checks don't keep threading through CCN-40 functions. | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: code-health-legacy) |

### Code Health — Engine (Omni-eval 2026-07-01)

| ID | Description | Status | Complexity | Source |

| BL-1006 | **Coverage thresholds exist only in @protopulse/graph's vitest config — the other 15 engine packages, including the contract-bearing erc/drc/export, have no coverage gate at all.** `packages/graph/vitest.config.ts` sets v8 coverage with per-file 100% thresholds on ops/apply/materialize/diff plus 95/95/90/95 global; every other package's vitest.config.ts (verified: emu, relay, erc, drc, export, ai, app, cli, content, cosim, parts, renderer, review, route, sim) is bare. BL-0265 (DONE, Wave 113) covers legacy-app CI coverage gates, a different surface — this is engine-only scope. Extend graph's pattern (modest global threshold + 100% on rule/verdict files) to erc/drc/export first so correctness regressions in those packages fail CI (`packages-ci.yml`) instead of drifting silently. Cross-reference: BL-0265. | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: code-health-engine) |
| BL-1007 | **DRC reaches app via `import.meta.glob` over a hardcoded relative path, and @protopulse/drc is missing from app's package.json dependencies despite the package existing.** `packages/app/src/drc/runner.ts:52` `import.meta.glob('../../../drc/src/index.ts')`, with a stale comment at :16-19 claiming 'packages/drc does not exist yet' — it does (678 non-test src lines, 38 test cases across drc.test.ts/geom.test.ts). `jq '.dependencies' packages/app/package.json` lists 13 `@protopulse/*` deps (including review/relay/cosim) but not drc, so npm workspaces can't see the real dependency. Sibling runners (emu/cosim/sim/review) already model the right pattern: declared dep + lazy `import('@protopulse/drc')`. Converting deletes ~30 lines of dead transitional scaffolding, including three copies of the 'DRC not available yet' error string (runner.ts:60,134,176). | OPEN | C1 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: code-health-engine) |
| BL-1008 | **@protopulse/content mixes node:fs loaders and browser-pure zod schemas in one entrypoint, forcing consumers into deep src imports.** `packages/content/src/index.ts` re-exports both pure schemas (from `./schemas.js`) and node-only loaders using `readFileSync` (from `./load.ts`, including `parseDeck`); `packages/content/package.json` has no exports map (main/types both `./src/index.ts`). App works around it with deep imports (`drc/runner.ts:1-3`, `puzzle/puzzles.ts:3-5` both import `@protopulse/content/src/schemas.js` directly) and a hand-duplicated 'browser-safe twin' of `parseDeck` (`drc/runner.ts:24-25`, `parseDeckJson`) that can drift from the canonical loader. Split the entrypoint into browser-safe (`./schemas`) and node (`./node`) exports with a proper exports map, then delete the twin and both deep imports. | OPEN | C1 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: code-health-engine) |
| BL-1009 | **packages/tsconfig.base.json puts DOM lib types into every package, so node-only packages (relay, emu, cli, content) typecheck stray browser globals.** `lib: ["ES2022", "DOM", "DOM.Iterable"]` is shared by every `packages/*/tsconfig.json` (verified ai/app/cli/content/cosim/drc/emu/erc/export/graph/parts/relay); relay is a node `ws` WebSocket server (`server.ts:1` `import { WebSocketServer } from 'ws'`) yet `document`/`window`/`fetch`/`localStorage` compile clean inside it. Drop DOM from the base and add a `tsconfig.dom.json` extended only by app and renderer — the two packages that legitimately touch DOM/WebGL — so a stray browser-global reference in relay or emu fails typecheck instead of crashing under node. | OPEN | C1 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: code-health-engine) |

### Meta-Layer / Dev Tooling & CI (Omni-eval 2026-07-01)

| ID | Description | Status | Complexity | Source |
|----|-------------|--------|------------|--------|
| BL-1010 | **npm-audit CI enforcement script exists but is never wired into a workflow — BL-0907's allowlist has no automated backstop** — `scripts/ci/check-npm-audit-policy.mjs` (severity-ranked allowlist checker) and `scripts/ci/npm-audit-allowlist.json` (per-package `until` expiry dates) both exist, purpose-built for CI gating; `grep -rn "check-npm-audit-policy|npm audit" .github/workflows/*.yml` returns nothing — not even in `tauri-build.yml`, which already runs an analogous supply-chain-check pattern for other checks. BL-0907 (OPEN) tracks 5 unpatchable HIGH genkit/opentelemetry vulns allowlisted until 2026-07-31, but nothing will fire when that date passes or when a new unlisted vuln lands via a future `npm install`. Wire `npm audit --json | node scripts/ci/check-npm-audit-policy.mjs ... scripts/ci/npm-audit-allowlist.json` into ci.yml so the expiry becomes a real gate. Related to, not a duplicate of, BL-0907 — that item tracks the vulnerabilities themselves; this is the unwired enforcement machinery. Cross-reference: BL-0907. | OPEN | C1 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: meta-layer) |
| BL-1011 | **Stop-hook auto-checkpoint commits everything under a non-descriptive 'Auto: N files' message with no size/content gating** — `settings.json` Stop hooks run `claudekit-hooks run create-checkpoint` unconditionally on every session Stop (alongside lint/test/todos and a stop-gate script), and `.claudekit/config.json` has no message-template override for it. `git log` shows commits like `ed105092 Auto: 117 files | 117 files changed, 10 insertions(+), 18715 deletions(-)` (dated 2026-07-01, verified) — a huge, undifferentiated diff under a generic message that gives no signal about what changed or why. Because checkpoint commits interleave with substantive work under identical generic messages, `git log`/`git bisect` can't distinguish a routine save from a commit that introduced or fixed a real regression without opening every diff. Options: (a) skip create-checkpoint when the diff only touches dirs already covered by a same-session descriptive commit, (b) summarize touched top-level dirs in the message (`Auto(client,packages/ai): 45 files`) instead of a bare count, or (c) exclude checkpoint commits from default `git log` views via a `.git-blame-ignore-revs`-style convention. | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: meta-layer) |
| BL-1012 | **Golden export fixtures have zero hook-level write protection, unlike package-lock.json/.env** — AGENTS.md/CLAUDE.md declare `tools/golden/fixtures/` a contract to "never re-freeze casually," but `.claude/hooks/protected-files.sh` enumerates `package-lock.json`, `replit.md/replit.nix`, `.env*` as blocked paths with zero mention of `golden` (verified via grep). `tools/golden/update-golden.ts` also has no confirmation gate, dry-run mode, or per-fixture targeting — it unconditionally rewrites every fixture's `expected.*` in one run. Add `tools/golden/fixtures/*/expected.*` and `design.ppx.json` to `protected-files.sh` (warn/confirm rather than hard-block, since `update-golden.ts` legitimately needs to write them) and give the script a `--fixture <name>` flag plus a printed pre-overwrite diff summary. | OPEN | C1 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: meta-layer) |
| BL-1013 | **No worktree-lifecycle or disk-budget guardrail automation** — `git worktree list` shows 7 worktrees under `.claude/worktrees/` totaling ~11.7GB, including `skill-audit-fixes` (7.5GB, 5.7GB of which is an orphaned `src-tauri/target/` — its actual audit work already merged per Memory `project_2026_06_12_slash_command_audit.md`) and two emu worktrees predating the 2026-06-23 ESP32-S3 completion-gate certification. `grep -rln "cargo clean|worktree.*prune|df -h" .claude/hooks/ scripts/*.sh` finds nothing — no automation ever checks merged-branch status, stale `target/` dirs, or disk headroom on this resource-constrained, earlyoom-guarded box (disk measured at 99% full, 6.5GB free of 439GB, on 2026-07-01 — a volatile snapshot, but symptomatic of the missing automation class). Add a periodic script flagging worktrees whose branch is `git branch --merged main` for removal, plus a SessionStart `df -h` threshold nudge. | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: meta-layer) |
| BL-1014 | **ROADMAP.md claims tauri:prepare-sidecars script is "missing" — it's shipped and hardened since 2026-06-13** — `ROADMAP.md:2140` (authored 2026-06-10, commit `26c9157e`) still reads "tauri-build.yml missing `tauri:prepare-sidecars` script," but `package.json` defines it and `tauri-build.yml:107` invokes it (`npm run tauri:prepare-sidecars -- --target ...`, both verified); the script's own git history shows it landed 2026-06-13 and was hardened twice since (`49d98f33` arduino-cli v1.4.1 + SHA256 verify, `602b5ae4` retry logic). Per this repo's own Documentation Rules ("ROADMAP.md is the ONLY place roadmap/status lives"), update or remove the stale sub-item so triage agents don't duplicate already-solved work. | OPEN | C1 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: meta-layer) |
| BL-1015 | **CI eslint runs with `--quiet`, silently hiding all warn-level rules including react-hooks/exhaustive-deps and jsx-a11y warn rules** — both `ci.yml` and `packages-ci.yml` run `eslint ... --quiet` (errors only, verified); `eslint.config.js` sets `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` (line 90) and several `jsx-a11y/*` rules (lines 65, 66, 75) to `'warn'`, meaning CI never surfaces them. `ROADMAP.md:2140`'s "~9.7k errors" lint-debt figure is unverified against this `--quiet` filter, conflating gating errors with total lint issues. Recommend dropping `--quiet` for a ratcheted `--max-warnings <N>` gate, or keeping `--quiet` but publishing a non-blocking `eslint --quiet=false` trend artifact so exhaustive-deps/a11y debt is at least visible instead of growing silently. | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: meta-layer) |
| BL-1016 | **`check-vault-primitive.sh` (architecture-conformance guard for the vault-consumption primitive) exists but no workflow invokes it** — the script's header documents itself as a "CI guard for 16-design-system Phase 8," enforcing that every vault-content consumer routes through `<VaultHoverCard>`/`<VaultExplainer>`/`useVaultQuickFetch`, with an explicit allowed-exceptions list; `grep -rn "check-vault-primitive" .github/workflows/*.yml` returns no matches (verified — only stale worktree copies reference it, also unwired). A new PR reintroducing a direct `useVaultNote`/`useVaultSearch` import would silently undo the Ars Contexta consumption-layer campaign's design intent. One-line fix: add `- run: bash scripts/ci/check-vault-primitive.sh` to `ci.yml`. | OPEN | C1 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: meta-layer) |
| BL-1017 | **No bundle-size budget/CI gate despite a deliberate manualChunks vendor-splitting strategy already in `vite.config.ts`** — `vite.config.ts:82-93` isolates `react-vendor` and leaves the rest to Vite's dynamic-import chunking with an explanatory tree-shaking comment (verified), showing bundle size is already a design concern; a grep for `bundle.*size|size-limit|bundlesize` across `package.json`/`.github/workflows/*.yml` returns no matches — nothing enforces a budget on `npm run build` output. Add a `size-limit` (or `vite-bundle-visualizer` + byte-threshold script) step to the build CI job, budgeted against current `dist/` output, so a heavy new dependency landing in the eager bundle doesn't regress load-time UX silently. | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: meta-layer) |
| BL-1018 | **`scripts/sync-skills.sh --check` (the canonical skills-drift lint) is never run automatically — CI or hook** — the script's header documents a 4-class drift model (IDENTICAL/ARSCONTEXTA/BREADBOARD/EXEMPT) purpose-built for machine gating (`--check` exits 1 on problems, verified), and Memory confirms it certified "clean — no drift" as of the 2026-07-01 BL-0905 cleanup — but `grep -n "sync-skills" .github/workflows/*.yml .claude/hooks/*.sh` finds zero automated invocation, so that certification is a one-time snapshot. With a 67-skill/60-command/29-hook surface that has already drifted twice (2026-06-11, 2026-07-01), add `scripts/sync-skills.sh --check` as a lightweight CI job or SessionStart nudge so drift is caught incrementally rather than needing a third full manual audit. | OPEN | C1 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: meta-layer) |

### Performance & Scalability (Omni-eval 2026-07-01)

| ID | Description | Status | Complexity | Source |
|----|-------------|--------|------------|--------|
| BL-1019 | **Zero performance/stress/benchmark tests across all 16 engine packages, and no documented component-count ceiling** — `fd -e bench.ts packages/` returns nothing; `rg -ril "large design|stress test|benchmark|1000 components|500 components|performance test" packages --glob '*.test.ts'` returns zero real matches (one unrelated substring hit in an ESP32-S3 touch-sensor test). `rg -n "performance.now()" packages -g '*.ts'` only surfaces real-time pacing in the emu/cosim runners, not benchmarking. ROADMAP.md/docs/FEATURE_MATURITY.md document no tested or intended ceiling for schematic node count, PCB pad/trace count, or op-log length — despite ~1,626 correctness tests, nobody has empirically found the engine's scaling cliff. | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: critic:large-design-performance-scalability) |
| BL-1020 | **WebGL2 renderer rebuilds the entire CPU-side vertex buffer for the whole scene on every single edit, with no culling** — `packages/renderer/src/gl/renderer.ts:268-335` `syncBuffers()` fires whenever `scene.version` changes (bumped on every mutation, `packages/renderer/src/scene.ts:56-67`) and unconditionally re-serializes every line/tri/hole-tri for ALL nodes into fresh `Float32Array`s, re-uploaded via `gl.bufferData(..., gl.STATIC_DRAW)` — even for a single symbol move. `rg -n "cull|viewport|visible|frustum"` finds only framebuffer-sizing `gl.viewport()` calls — no spatial index, no frustum test. `STATIC_DRAW` is also the wrong usage hint for buffers rewritten on nearly every edit. On a design with thousands of symbols/traces, one drag would re-serialize the entire board on every changed frame. | OPEN | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: critic:large-design-performance-scalability) |
| BL-1021 | **Legacy schematic canvas omits React Flow's `onlyRenderVisibleElements`, the library's documented fix for exactly this scale problem** — `client/src/components/circuit-editor/SchematicCanvas.tsx:836-860` instantiates `<ReactFlow>` with a long prop list that never sets `onlyRenderVisibleElements`. Per Context7-verified React Flow docs, this flag limits rendering to nodes/edges actually visible in the viewport; without it, React Flow mounts a real DOM node (and custom component, e.g. `SchematicInstanceNode`) for every instance/net-label/annotation regardless of what's on-screen — a documented perf cliff, not a hypothesis. No component-count ceiling is documented anywhere. | OPEN | C1 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: critic:large-design-performance-scalability) |

### Security & Robustness (Omni-eval 2026-07-01)

| ID | Description | Status | Complexity | Source |
|----|-------------|--------|------------|--------|
| BL-1022 | **Global SPICE model catalog is writable by any authenticated user with no ownership gate and no rate limit** — `server/routes/spice-models.ts:45-78` (`POST /api/spice-models`) and `:83-157` (`/import`) have zero ownership/role checks beyond the blanket session gate, and no rate limiter (unlike `settings.ts`'s `validateKeyLimiter` or `admin.ts`'s `adminRateLimiter`). `storage.getSpiceModels`/`createSpiceModel` are global, non-project-scoped tables shared by every user. `sanitizeSpiceDirective` (`server/spice-import.ts:61`) mitigates content-injection in the directive text but not integrity/availability of the shared catalog — any user can pollute a resource every other user's simulations depend on. | OPEN | C1 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: security-robustness) |

### Hardware & Parts Breadth (Omni-eval 2026-07-01)

| ID | Description | Status | Complexity | Source |
|----|-------------|--------|------------|--------|
| BL-1023 | **Legacy-import `PART_RULES` table never grew past the original M1 seed — the large majority of the current seed catalog is unreachable via `protopulse import-legacy`** — `packages/cli/src/legacy.ts:105-123` `PART_RULES` covers exactly 15 distinct part ids (bat54s, 1n4148, 1n5819, tvs, 555, 2n3904, ao3400/nmos, led, capacitor(-electrolytic), resistor, pushbutton, battery, usbc, header) — the original comment-documented M1 seed set. Verified against the current catalog (`grep -n "id: '" packages/parts/src/seed/index.ts | wc -l` = 62 parts total): only 15 map, leaving 47 parts (~76%) — including esp32s3, all sensors (tmp36, bme280, mpu6050, ky-family), RTCs (ds1302/ds3231), motor drivers (l298n/l293d/tb6612fng), displays (max7219/lcd1602/tm1637), and every Arduino/ESP board — with no `mapLegacyPart()` match. `importLegacy()` skips unmapped parts with no guessed pinouts (`legacy.ts:22-23`), so any legacy design using a common maker part added since the M1 seed silently drops connectivity on import. The legacy→engine migration is an explicit ROADMAP milestone (v0.6→v0.7); this is a pure regression-by-omission as the seed library grew 15→62 parts without the mapping table following. | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: hardware-parts-breadth) |
| BL-1024 | **No voltage-regulator part in the engine seed library despite legacy already having a verified AMS1117-3.3 reference part** — `grep -inE "regulat|lm7805|ams1117|lm317" packages/parts/src/seed/index.ts` returns zero `definePart` hits (only prose comments about onboard regulators on modules). Legacy already models one with real specs: `shared/standard-library.ts:337` — AMS1117-3.3, Advanced Monolithic Systems, SOT-223, 6.5×3.5×1.6mm — plus a dedicated `client/src/components/circuit-editor/breadboard-components/RegulatorSvg.tsx` renderer with no engine-side equivalent. Voltage regulation (5V→3.3V, battery→logic rail) is in essentially every non-trivial Arduino/ESP32 build; the legacy catalog already proves the exact spec needed, making this a low-effort, low-risk port. | OPEN | C1 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: hardware-parts-breadth) |
| BL-1025 | **No DHT11/DHT22 temperature-humidity sensor part despite it being a rite-of-passage beginner Arduino sensor** — `grep -inE "dht11|dht22" packages/parts/src/seed/index.ts` returns no matches. Legacy already carries verified specs: `shared/standard-library.ts:424-425` — DHT11 (Aosong, 0-50°C/20-80%RH, single-wire, ±2°C/±5%RH) and DHT22 (-40-80°C/0-100%RH, ±0.5°C/±2%RH), with manufacturer, MPN, and dimensions. Ubiquitous in tutorials; the exact reference data already exists in `standard-library.ts`, making this a near-zero-research port. | OPEN | C1 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: hardware-parts-breadth) |
| BL-1026 | **No WS2812B/NeoPixel addressable LED part or emu/cosim device model** — `grep -inE "ws2812|neopixel" packages/parts/src/seed/index.ts packages/emu/src/i2c-devices.ts packages/emu/src/spi-devices.ts` returns zero hits in all three files. Legacy's BL-0619 (DONE) only shipped client-side visual-state rendering for the legacy simulator, not an engine part or a single-wire protocol device model in @protopulse/emu. Addressable RGB LEDs are near-universal in maker projects and would exercise a bit-banged single-wire timing protocol architecturally distinct from the engine's existing I2C/SPI device models — a genuinely new co-sim capability, not just another catalog entry. | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: hardware-parts-breadth) |
| BL-1027 | **No SSD1306 I2C OLED display part or device model — the most common hobbyist graphic display is missing from both the parts catalog and the 3-device I2C co-sim surface** — `packages/emu/src/i2c-devices.ts:144-155`'s `I2C_DEVICES_BY_PART_ID` maps exactly 3 devices (mpu6050, bme280, ds3231), no ssd1306/oled entry; `grep -inE "ssd1306|oled" packages/parts/src/seed/index.ts` returns nothing (existing displays are lcd1602 character LCD, max7219 8x8 matrix, tm1637 7-seg — no graphic OLED). SSD1306 OLEDs are the default graphic display for nearly every ESP32/Arduino maker project; it would also be the engine's first WRITE-heavy I2C device model, since the existing 3 are read-only sensors. | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: hardware-parts-breadth) |
| BL-1028 | **No hobby servo motor part or PWM-actuator device model — a rite-of-passage Arduino actuator with zero engine representation** — `grep -rliE "\bservo\b" packages/parts packages/emu` returns nothing. Legacy references servos across `shared/arduino-examples.ts`, `shared/starter-circuits.ts`, and the Arduino firmware templates. @protopulse/emu's device abstraction (`i2c-devices.ts`, `spi-devices.ts`) only models bus-slave peripherals — there's no PWM-consuming actuator device model at all, so even after adding a servo part, cosim would have nowhere to plug it in. Servo control is one of the earliest 'make something move' projects for beginners and a common robotics-starter-kit component. | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: hardware-parts-breadth) |
| BL-1029 | **PartClass taxonomy collapses two-thirds of the seed catalog into a single 'ic' bucket, unlike legacy's dedicated componentType enum** — `packages/parts/src/types.ts`'s `PartClass` union (~lines 76-88) has only resistor/capacitor/inductor/diode/led/transistor/ic/connector/switch/power/battery/other — no sensor/display/motor/regulator/module/mcu-board values. Measured distribution (`grep -oP "class: '\K[a-z_]+" packages/parts/src/seed/index.ts | sort | uniq -c`, total 62 parts): 41 of 62 (66%) are class `ic`, lumping sensors (bme280, mpu6050, tmp36), displays (lcd1602, max7219, tm1637), RTCs (ds1302, ds3231), motor drivers (l298n, l293d, tb6612fng), and dev boards (esp32s3, arduino-*, nodemcu-*) into one undifferentiated category. Legacy already solved this with `shared/schema.ts:569` (`voltage_regulator`, `mosfet`, `jfet`, etc.) plus per-family SVG renderers. As the catalog grows past 62 parts (the emu campaign's stated breadth direction), a flat `ic` bucket makes any category-based browse/filter UI in the palette unusable without reinventing classification from scratch. | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: hardware-parts-breadth) |
| BL-1030 | **@protopulse/emu has zero coverage for ATmega2560, ESP8266, and classic dual-core ESP32 — parts already exist in the seed catalog but cannot run firmware or cosim** — `packages/emu/src/*.ts` contains only `atmega328p.ts`, `rp2040.ts`, `esp32s3.ts` cores (`grep -rn "atmega2560\|esp8266" packages/emu/src/*.ts` returns zero hits). Yet the seed library ships schematic-only representations of these exact chips: `arduino-mega2560`, `nodemcu-esp8266` + `esp8266ex` bare chip, `nodemcu-esp32s` classic (all verified present via `id:` grep). Comparable products (Wokwi) support ATmega2560 and classic/S2/S3/RISC-V ESP32 variants per its docs. Users who pick the Mega or a classic ESP32/ESP8266 board get a part that looks real on the schematic but silently can't run firmware or participate in co-sim — a 'looks real, doesn't work' trap. False-flagged as a BL-0613 duplicate: BL-0613 (DONE) only expanded the legacy compile-firmware board REGISTRY (PlatformIO-style), not @protopulse/emu's instruction-level emulation — cross-reference only, since its DONE status could otherwise create a false impression this gap was addressed. Cross-reference: BL-0613. | OPEN | C5 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: hardware-parts-breadth) |
| BL-1031 | **STM32 (ARM Cortex-M) architecture entirely unsupported in @protopulse/emu** — `packages/emu/src/` contains only `atmega328p.ts`, `rp2040.ts`, `esp32s3.ts`; `grep -rinE "stm32" packages/emu/src packages/parts/src/seed/index.ts` returns zero hits — no ARM core, no STM32 part. Comparable products (Wokwi) ship STM32C031/L031/F103C8 as a fourth architecture family alongside AVR/RP2040/Xtensa per its docs. STM32 Blue Pill is one of the most common boards makers graduate to after Arduino; without an ARM Cortex-M core the engine's co-sim/time-travel-debugging story hits a wall for that segment. False-flagged as a BL-0613 duplicate: BL-0613 (DONE, Wave 138) only expanded the legacy PlatformIO-style compile board registry, not instruction-level emulation — its closure creates a false impression this specific gap was already addressed; cross-reference only. Cross-reference: BL-0613. | OPEN | C5 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: hardware-parts-breadth) |
| BL-1032 | **Sourcing catalog schema has zero seeded data** — `@protopulse/content`'s `CatalogEntrySchema`/`CatalogSchema` (packages/content/src/schemas.ts:56-77, LCSC/MPN/manufacturer/package/JLC-class fields) and the `parseCatalog`/`loadCatalogFile` loaders (packages/content/src/load.ts:54-72) are fully built, but no catalog data file exists anywhere in the repo (`find packages/content -iname "*catalog*"`/`"*sourc*"` return nothing). A part with no sourcing entry can't feed BOM→procurement/JLCPCB-quote flows, blocking the parts-breadth work from being end-to-end useful. | OPEN | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: hardware-parts-breadth) |
| BL-1033 | **Breadboard Lab supports only one fixed board size (BB830)** — `client/src/lib/circuit-editor/breadboard-constants.ts:26-50` hardcodes BB830 geometry (830 tie points, source busboard.com/BB830) as the sole board model; `grep -rniE "BB400|BB170|half.?size|mini breadboard"` across `client/src/lib` and `client/src/components/circuit-editor` finds nothing, and `client/src/lib/multi-board.ts` (BL-0454) is scoped to multi-PCB systems, not breadboard tiling. Real maker workflows routinely use half-size boards or join multiple full-size ones; a single fixed size caps physical-fit fidelity. | OPEN | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: hardware-parts-breadth) |

'active' : ''}` only (packages/app/src/styles.css:54-58 changes color alone); `ProfessorPanel.tsx:37-50`'s near-identical "depth dial" in the same package does it correctly with `role="radiogroup"`/`role="radio"`/`aria-checked`. Related to BL-0768 (same toggle-state-not-exposed pattern, but scoped to the legacy Breadboard Lab's DRC/connectivity-explainer toggles) — this is the same anti-pattern recurring in the new engine's primary toolbar, a different surface. Cross-reference: BL-0768. | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: a11y-static) |
| BL-1036 | **Inspector's Value field (primary per-component edit control) has no accessible name** — `packages/app/src/panels/Inspector.tsx:141-152`'s `<dt>Value</dt><dd><input placeholder="e.g. 10k" .../></dd>` relies on `<dl>/<dt>/<dd>` structure, which has no programmatic label-association semantics, plus a placeholder that isn't a reliable accessible name. The net-rename input 60 lines earlier in the same file (Inspector.tsx:90, `aria-label={net ${net.name} name}`) already shows the correct fix. | OPEN | C1 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: a11y-static) |
| BL-1037 | **ConceptViewer overlay declares `role="dialog"` with none of the supporting dialog behavior** — `packages/app/src/panels/ConceptViewer.tsx:55-68` has no `aria-modal`, no focus trap, no initial-focus move, no focus restore, and no Escape-to-close (zero matches for `aria-modal`/`focus(`/`Escape` in the file; no focus-trap dependency in packages/app/package.json, and legacy's `@radix-ui/react-dialog` isn't used here). It's the only overlay/dialog surface in the whole engine app, so a keyboard user can currently tab out of a control that's supposed to be modal, and screen readers get no dialog-mode announcement. | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: a11y-static) |
| BL-1038 | **Async sim/DRC/co-sim/review status renders as plain text with no live region, in both engine and legacy** — packages/app/src/panels/SimPanel.tsx:686-693 (`{busy && <p>Run {done}/{total}…</p>}`), DrcPanel.tsx, ReviewPanel.tsx, and CosimPanel.tsx all follow the same pattern with zero `aria-live` hits across all four files; legacy's `client/src/components/simulation/SimulationPanel.tsx:816-824` has the identical gap. Runs can take several seconds; a screen-reader user gets no indication a run started, is progressing, or finished — the fix is already proven correct at `packages/app/src/App.tsx:283` (`role="status"` on the status-flash span). | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: a11y-static) |
| BL-1039 | **AI chat streaming indicator has no aria-live region — streamed tokens are silent to screen readers** — `client/src/components/panels/chat/StreamingIndicator.tsx:9-37` renders live-streamed response text with no `aria-live`/`role="status"`/`role="log"`, and `ChatPanel.tsx:253-256` mounts it with no wrapping live region either. The AI chat is a flagship interaction (six-persona crew); a screen-reader user currently only discovers the assistant's response once streaming finishes, and only by manually re-navigating. | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: a11y-static) |
| BL-1040 | **Engine app's global button/input chrome fails WCAG 1.4.11 non-text contrast (measured, not inferred)** — `packages/app/src/styles.css`: `input[type='text']` (:60-68) sets `background: var(--bg)`, identical to the page background `--bg: #0b0e14` (contrast 1.0:1); `button` (:35-43)'s `--bg-raised` (#121724) against page bg measures 1.08:1 by the WCAG relative-luminance formula (hand-verified); the sole differentiator, the 1px `border: var(--border)` (#232b3d), measures 1.37:1. All three are well under the 3:1 minimum for UI-component boundaries, across every button/input in all 17 panels of the new (not-yet-shipping) editor. Fix is a CSS-custom-property token change (raise `--border` lightness / differentiate `--bg-raised` from `--bg`), which cascades everywhere since these aren't per-component hardcoded values. | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: a11y-static) |
| BL-1041 | **39 low-opacity `text-muted-foreground/20`-`/40` Tailwind modifiers push legacy small text to 1.6-2.5:1 contrast, well under WCAG AA** — measured via the WCAG relative-luminance formula: default theme `--color-muted-foreground` (client/src/index.css) is 7.45:1 at full opacity but drops to 2.04:1 at 40% and 1.62:1 at 30%; the high-contrast/OLED variant (index.css:382) fares only slightly better (2.48:1/1.88:1). `rg -o 'text-muted-foreground/(2[0-9]|3[0-9]|40)' client/src -g '*.tsx'` finds 39 matches across 28 files, e.g. ImportHistoryPanel.tsx:91 and DesignDiffPanel.tsx:193/238 on real 10px informational text. BL-0868 (OPEN) scoped the same underlying pattern narrowly to breadboard decorative rails/tie-points; this shows the opacity-modifier contrast risk is systemic across 28 panels, well beyond that scope, alongside BL-0324 (DONE Wave 108). Cross-reference: BL-0868. | OPEN | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: a11y-static) |

Cross-reference: BL-0378. | OPEN | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: docs-onboarding) |
