### Offline / PWA Story (Omni-eval 2026-07-01)

| ID | Description | Status | Complexity | Source |
|----|-------------|--------|------------|--------|
| BL-1049 | **Legacy client ships a full offline-cache service worker but no web app manifest — not actually installable as a PWA** — `client/index.html` has no `<link rel="manifest">` tag (full file read, verified); repo-wide search for `webmanifest`/`manifest.json` finds zero hits outside unrelated `.ppx`/node_modules matches. `client/public/` has only a single 128x128 favicon, no maskable/192/512 icon set, and `rg -n "beforeinstallprompt" client/src` returns zero results, so `canInstall`/`setInstallable` in `pwa-manager.ts:583-587` are dead setters never fed by a real browser install-prompt event. Meanwhile `client/public/sw.js` (285 lines, 5 caching strategies) is fully wired from `client/src/main.tsx:57` in production. Add `manifest.webmanifest` + a proper icon set (~1-2 hours) so the app gets the install/discoverability benefit its SW maintenance cost already pays for. | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: critic:offline-pwa-story) |
| BL-1050 | **Two parallel, non-interoperating 'offline sync' implementations coexist — one is a UI-orphaned fake** — `client/src/lib/pwa-manager.ts:472-515` `triggerSync()` just flips `c.synced = true` locally with a comment "In a real implementation, this would POST pending changes to the server" (no fetch call); `checkForUpdate()` (294-301) always returns `false`; `skipWaiting()` (304-307) is an empty stub. This is a second, in-memory/localStorage (`STORAGE_KEY='protopulse-pwa-manager'`) "offline changes" system that duplicates but never talks to the real IndexedDB queue in offline-sync.ts. `rg -n "usePwaManager" client/src -g'*.tsx'` finds zero consumers outside pwa-manager.ts itself — whoever picks up the offline story next needs to know which of the two is 'the' queue before wiring anything. | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: critic:offline-pwa-story) |
| BL-1051 | **New engine editor (packages/app) has no PWA/offline-shell story, and the regression vs legacy is untracked** — `packages/app/index.html` has no `<link rel="manifest">` and no SW registration; `rg -n "serviceWorker" packages/app/src` and `find packages/app -iname sw.js -o -iname '*manifest*'` both return nothing. `packages/app/src/state/persistence.ts:44-51` debounce-autosaves the design to `localStorage` (key `pp-design`), so live editing has no network dependency once loaded, but a cold offline page load has no cached app shell to serve — unlike legacy's `sw.js` cache-first app-shell. `rg -i "pwa|service worker|offline|installable|manifest" ROADMAP.md docs/FEATURE_MATURITY.md` finds nothing — this capability regression on the acknowledged migration target is undocumented. | OPEN | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: critic:offline-pwa-story) |

### Backup & Disaster Recovery (Omni-eval 2026-07-01)

| ID | Description | Status | Complexity | Source |
|----|-------------|--------|------------|--------|
| BL-1052 | **Backup automation is a documentation-only cron snippet — the runbook's RPO promise isn't backed by anything actually scheduled** — `docs/backup-runbook.md:83-99` instructs an operator to manually add `0 2 * * * .../backup.sh` to their crontab; no `.github/workflows/*.yml` schedules it. The Overview table (`docs/backup-runbook.md:7-13`) states "RPO ... Daily cron = up to 24h of data loss" as settled fact with no caveat that the daily cron is a suggested manual step, not a running schedule. | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: critic:backup-disaster-recovery) |
| BL-1053 | **Relay op-log persistence is opt-in via an unverified env var — in-flight collaborative ops vanish on crash unless PP_RELAY_DATA happens to be set** — `packages/relay/src/main.ts:14-21` only wires `createFileStorage(dataDir)` when `PP_RELAY_DATA` is set; the default (`storage: undefined`, `packages/relay/src/server.ts:47-53`) is memory-only `Map<string, Room>`. This is a deliberate design ("the relay carries, it never owns" — server.ts:15-25) that depends entirely on every client's local copy being durable and staying connected long enough to receive every op — but there's no readiness check or CI test asserting `PP_RELAY_DATA` is actually set in any deployed environment. | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: critic:backup-disaster-recovery) |

### Licensing & Legal Compliance (Omni-eval 2026-07-01)

| ID | Description | Status | Complexity | Source |
|----|-------------|--------|------------|--------|
| BL-1054 | **Zero automated license-compliance scanning anywhere in CI**, verified against installed packages — `grep -il "licens" .github/workflows/*.yml` returns 0 matches across ci.yml/packages-ci.yml/tauri-build.yml, and no package.json declares a license-checker/licensee/spdx devDependency. A manual `npx license-checker --summary` run surfaced real, currently-uncaught signals: 17 packages reporting UNLICENSED (the 16 `@protopulse/*` packages + tools-golden) and 1 dual-licensed `(MIT OR GPL-3.0-or-later)` runtime dependency (`jszip@3.10.1`, used in `server/export/fritzing-exporter.ts`, `server/export/odb-plus-plus-generator.ts`, `server/export/fzz-handler.ts`, `server/component-export.ts`, `server/google-workspace.ts`). No pure GPL/AGPL/SSPL package found today, but nothing would flag one landing via a future `npm install`. Add a `license-checker --failOn 'GPL;AGPL;SSPL'` step to ci.yml/packages-ci.yml. | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: critic:licensing-legal-compliance) |
| BL-1055 | **`packages/sim` bundles a compiled BSD-3-Clause ngspice-WASM binary via `eecircuit-engine` into the engine app bundle with zero third-party attribution and unpinned, unverified build flags** — `packages/sim/package.json:15` depends on `eecircuit-engine: "^1.7.0"` (caret range, no git tags on the upstream repo so the exact build commit can't be pinned); `packages/sim/src/engine.ts:1` directly imports `Simulation` from it and `packages/app/src/sim/sim.worker.ts:1` wires it into `@protopulse/app`. ngspice is primarily BSD-3-Clause but has documented GPL-contamination risk (XSPICE 'table' codemodel, GPL readline) if built wrong; the installed build correctly passes `--disable-xspice --with-readline=no` (verified against upstream's `Docker/run.sh`) and a grep of the compiled `.mjs` for GPL markers returns 0 matches — so today's state is clean. But there is no `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES`/`NOTICE` file anywhere in the repo (BSD-3-Clause requires reproducing the copyright notice in binary-form redistribution), and the caret range means a future bump could silently re-enable GPL-risk flags with no compliance re-check. Pin the exact version, add `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` capturing ngspice's BSD-3-Clause + eecircuit-engine's MIT notices, and record the verified build flags so a future bump that changes them gets caught. | OPEN | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: critic:licensing-legal-compliance) |

### Data Format & Schema-Versioning (Omni-eval 2026-07-01)

| ID | Description | Status | Complexity | Source |
|----|-------------|--------|------------|--------|
| BL-1056 | **.ppx `version` field is write-only decoration — zero migration/upgrade code path exists in packages/graph** — `PPX_FORMAT_VERSION = 1` (`packages/graph/src/store/serialize.ts:95`) is stamped on every write (`fs-store.ts:83`, `persistence.ts:37/128`, `legacy.ts:376`, `share.test.ts:16`) but never read back or compared: `rg -ni 'migrat|upgrade|version\s*===|version\s*<|version\s*!=='` across `packages/graph/src/*.ts` returns zero matches. Both `manifestSchema` and `designBundleSchema` validate `version` only as `z.number().int().positive()` — any positive integer passes identically, nothing branches on its value. The op-log IS the design; the moment the op vocabulary changes and the version bumps to 2, every existing save file becomes unparseable with no bridge written. | OPEN | C4 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: critic:ppx-schema-versioning-migration) |
| BL-1057 | **opBodySchema is a fully closed 33-kind discriminated union with no unknown-op passthrough — any vocabulary change is a hard parse failure** — `packages/graph/src/ops.ts:322-361`: `opBodySchema` unions a `z.discriminatedUnion('kind', [...])` of 33 enumerated op kinds plus `zBatch`, with no catch-all branch. `decodeOpl` (`packages/graph/src/store/serialize.ts:20-27`) calls `.parse()` per line with no try/skip-unknown logic, so one op of an unrecognized `kind` throws and aborts the whole load. Even a minimal, additive vocabulary change makes every prior `.ppx` op-log segment fail validation on a newer engine build — no preserve-and-skip mechanism exists, the standard technique for op-log/CRDT schema evolution. | OPEN | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: critic:ppx-schema-versioning-migration) |
| BL-1058 | **No cross-version regression fixture exists anywhere in the suite — every .ppx test fixture is pinned to the current PPX_FORMAT_VERSION** — `packages/graph/src/store/store.test.ts` (5 describe blocks) uses a literal `version: 1` (line 76); `packages/app/src/state/share.test.ts:16` imports the live `PPX_FORMAT_VERSION`; `packages/cli/src/cli.test.ts:78` uses `version: 1`; all `tools/golden/fixtures/*/design.ppx.json` are regenerated via `tools/golden/update-golden.ts:42` (`version: 1`) against current HEAD. No frozen fixture from an older format version exists anywhere, and the suite's two negative tests (`store.test.ts:85-86` "rejects bundles with a bad shape", `:151-160` "rejects a manifest whose head is not a branch") both assert `.toThrow()` — the suite actively verifies strict rejection, never graceful upgrade. Once migration code is written, there is currently no regression harness that would catch a backward-compatibility break. | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: critic:ppx-schema-versioning-migration) |
| BL-1059 | **Engine's only KiCad export is a connectivity-only netlist stub; the real editable-native-format escape hatch exists solely in legacy code slated for retirement** — `packages/export/src/` contains only `bom-csv.ts, excellon.ts, gerber.ts, kicad-netlist.ts, panel.ts, pcb-common.ts, pick-place.ts, sexpr.ts` — `exportKicadNetlist` (`kicad-netlist.ts:37`) emits component/value/footprint/net list only, no placement/traces/vias/schematic geometry. No Eagle exporter exists in packages/export at all. By contrast legacy has `server/export/kicad/board.ts` (full `.kicad_pcb`), `server/export/kicad/symbols.ts` (374 lines, schematic symbols), and `server/export/eagle-exporter.ts` (1291 lines, native Eagle) — none ported to the engine. Per AGENTS.md the engine is the eventual sole product; if these aren't ported before legacy retirement, any design saved purely as `.ppx` can be fabricated but not taken as editable source to a competing EDA tool. | OPEN | C4 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: critic:ppx-schema-versioning-migration) |

| ID | Description | Status | Complexity | Source |

| BL-0905 | **Clean committed `.claude/` cruft** — `git rm -r` the tracked `.claude/Untitled Folder/` (113 files — a full duplicate of the commands + skills trees) and the `settings*.bak` files (`settings.json.pre-stop-hook-cleanup.bak`, `settings.local.json.pre-skill-cleanup.bak`). Duplicated instruction surfaces are the "drifting duplicate context" anti-pattern and inflate the apparent skill/command count (true surface: 67 skills / 60 commands / 29 hooks). **Jurisdiction guard:** delete only the stray duplicate copies — leave every canonical Codex-owned `pp-*`/`pp-nlm-*` skill/command/hook untouched. Pair with `scripts/sync-skills.sh --check` as a drift lint. **DONE (2026-07-01):** verified before deleting — 19 of the 113 files *differed* from canonical, but git history proved Untitled Folder was a one-shot 2026-06-22 snapshot never touched again (canonical updated 06-23+; diffs = stale whitespace; zero Untitled-only files) → `git rm -r` (113 files) + both `settings*.bak`. Drift lint then surfaced 3 pre-existing problems, all fixed in the same pass: `meta` SKILL.md given valid non-invocable frontmatter, dangling `claude-automation-recommender` symlink (target gone since Feb) removed, empty untracked `gemini-cli-maestro` husk cleaned. `scripts/sync-skills.sh --check` now reports **clean — no drift, no lint findings**. Codex-owned canonical `pp-*` surfaces untouched. | DONE | C1 | AI-first audit 2026-06-28 |
| BL-0906 | **Reduce repo-root noise** — 26 tracked `CODEX_*` / `COLLAB_*` / `CLAUDE_RESPONSE_*` / `RALPH-*` point-in-time history files sit at repo root and push real entry points down the first-scan listing an agent reads. AGENTS.md already classifies them as history → relocate to `docs/handoffs/` or `archive/`. Keep `AGENTS.md`/`README.md`/`ROADMAP.md`/`ARCHITECTURE.md`/`DESIGN.md` at root. **DONE (2026-07-01):** all 26 `CODEX_*`/`COLLAB_*`/`CLAUDE_RESPONSE_*`/`RALPH-*` files `git mv`'d to `docs/handoffs/` (kept together — they cross-reference each other by bare name, so co-location preserves the links). Living-doc references updated in the same pass: AGENTS.md Documentation Rules row now points at `docs/handoffs/` + states "new handoff artifacts land there, never at root"; ROADMAP off-vision bullet marked done; `scribe/breadboard-lab/RESEARCH.md` source pointer fixed. Point-in-time references (audits/plans/collab) untouched per rules. Root residue: 0. | DONE | C2 | AI-first audit 2026-06-28 |

### Breadboard UX — Fritzing Parity (Wave 66 Competitive Audit)

| ID | Description | Status | Complexity | Source |
|----|-------------|--------|------------|--------|
| BL-0590 | **Photorealistic breadboard component SVGs** — Fritzing renders components as photorealistic images: resistors with real color bands, ICs with part number markings, LEDs with colored lens shapes, capacitors with correct proportions. ProtoPulse uses schematic-style abstractions on the breadboard. "Looks like my real circuit" is the primary reason beginners love Fritzing — it makes the diagram instantly recognizable against the physical hardware. | DONE | C3 | Wave 69 |
| BL-0591 | **Wire color coding in breadboard** — Right-click any breadboard wire to assign a color (red=power, black=ground, yellow=signal, etc.). Wire colors persist and are exported to diagrams. Standard electronics convention that makes breadboard diagrams readable at a glance. Every Fritzing tutorial uses this. | DONE | C3 | Wave 69 |
| BL-0592 | **Breadboard connected-row highlight on hover** — Hovering over any breadboard hole highlights every other hole electrically connected to it (the full row on the top/bottom half, or the connected power rail). Teaches beginners how breadboards work while also functioning as a visual continuity checker during layout. Very low implementation cost, very high educational value. | DONE | C3 | Wave 69 |
| BL-0593 | **Bendable/rubber-band component legs** — Bezier leg paths from component body to breadboard holes, 7 component types, metallic gradients, BendableLegRenderer SVG. | DONE (Wave 87) | C3 | Wave 66 / Fritzing |
| BL-0594 | **Part family swapping via Inspector** — Components with the same "family" property (e.g. all resistors) show a value dropdown in the Inspector panel. Swap a 100Ω resistor for a 1kΩ without deleting and re-placing — all wire connections are preserved. Avoids the most common tedious operation in breadboard design. | DONE (Wave 74) | C3 | Wave 66 / Fritzing |
| BL-0595 | **Arbitrary angle rotation** — Inspector rotation field should accept any angle (e.g. 30°, 45°, 135°), not just 90° multiples. Required for angled connector placements, off-axis through-hole components, and decorative layouts. | DONE (Wave 73) | C2 | Wave 66 / Fritzing |
| BL-0596 | **Mystery part (generic black-box placeholder)** — A configurable placeholder component with user-set pin count, pin labels, and a blank body. Used when a specific part isn't in the library yet. Allows circuit design to continue without blocking on part creation. Common in breadboard prototyping where custom modules need representation. | DONE (Wave 72) | C2 | Wave 66 / Fritzing |
| BL-0597 | **Wire T-junction forking** — Alt+drag on any wire bend point creates a new branching wire (T-junction). Currently wires must be routed from a component pin; mid-wire branching is not possible. T-junctions are essential for power distribution nets where multiple components share a rail. | DONE (verified Wave 106) | C4 | Wave 66 / Fritzing |

| BL-0505 | **Push-shove visual feedback** — `push-shove-engine.ts` computes pushed geometries but the result is not visualized during routing. Show the "pushed" trace outlines in a highlight color before the user commits the route. | DONE (Wave 75) | C3 | Wave 64 audit |
| BL-0506 | **Unrouted net completion status** — Show a count of unrouted connections (total vs routed) in the PCB toolbar, and a per-net completion indicator in the net browser. Standard autorouter UX feedback. | DONE | C3 | Wave 69 |
| BL-0507 | **DRC solder mask / paste / assembly rules** — The PCB DRC checker covers clearance/annular/trace rules but has no rules for solder mask expansion, paste aperture, or assembly courtyard clearances. Add at least 3 rule types for these. | DONE (Wave 72) | C3 | Wave 64 audit |
| BL-0508 | **Via aspect ratio DRC rule** — Validate that hole diameter / board thickness ratio meets the fab constraint (typically 1:6 max). Currently via DRC only checks annular ring; aspect ratio is a common reason Gerbers get rejected. | DONE (Wave 73) | C2 | Wave 64 audit |
| BL-0509 | **Impedance-aware trace width enforcement** — When a net has an impedance target set (via net class), auto-suggest or enforce the trace width required to hit that impedance given the stackup. Currently impedance is computed but not fed back into routing. | DONE (Wave 75) | C4 | Wave 64 audit |
| BL-0510 | **Diff pair length matching automation** — `diff-pair-meander.ts` can generate serpentine segments but there is no UI or automation to trigger length-matching to a target delta. Users must manually invoke via AI. Add a "Match lengths" button in the diff pair toolbar. | DONE (Wave 75) | C4 | Wave 64 audit |

### Simulation UX — TinkerCAD Parity (Wave 66 Competitive Audit)

> TinkerCAD's simulation is less powerful than ProtoPulse's (no AC, no Monte Carlo, no real SPICE) but it FEELS 10x more powerful because you SEE results on the circuit. These items close that perception gap.

| ID | Description | Status | Complexity | Source |
|----|-------------|--------|------------|--------|
| BL-0619 | **Component visual state rendering during simulation** — During simulation, components should show their physical state visually on the canvas: LED brightness proportional to current (CSS glow effect), motor/servo SVG animated at simulated RPM/angle, 7-segment displays show lit segments, LCDs render text strings from simulated output, NeoPixels show per-pixel colors. This is distinct from BL-0128 (current flow animation overlay) — that's wires; this is the components themselves. TinkerCAD's killer feature. | DONE (Wave 83) | C4 | Wave 66 / TinkerCAD |
| BL-0620 | **Unified "Start Simulation" play button with auto-detection** — TinkerCAD has a single green "Start Simulation" button. ProtoPulse requires choosing DC/AC/transient, configuring parameters, then reading separate result panels. Add a top-level play button that auto-detects the appropriate simulation type from the circuit topology and shows results visually on the canvas. Advanced users can still access full parameter control. | DONE (Wave 83) | C4 | Wave 66 / TinkerCAD |
| BL-0621 | **Interactive component controls during simulation** — During a running simulation, components should be interactable: click a button/switch to toggle it, drag a potentiometer knob to change resistance, click an LED to see its current node voltage. Changes feed into the live simulation in real time. This is what makes TinkerCAD feel like a living circuit. Requires pausing the sim, applying the state change, and resuming. | DONE | C3 | Wave 69 |
| BL-0622 | **Sensor environmental sliders during simulation** — When simulating circuits with temperature sensors (NTC, LM35), light sensors (LDR, photodiode), or distance sensors (HC-SR04), show a slider in the simulation panel that lets the user set the environmental input (0–100°C, 0–100k lux, 0–400cm). The slider drives `analogRead()` / sensor output voltage in the simulation. | DONE (Wave 82) | C3 | Wave 66 / TinkerCAD |
| BL-0623 | **Audio output from buzzer/speaker during simulation** — When the simulated circuit drives a buzzer or speaker, produce actual audio through the browser's Web Audio API at the simulated frequency and duty cycle. `tone(pin, 440)` produces an A4 note. Makes the simulation feel alive and is directly useful for debugging audio projects. | DONE (Wave 73) | C2 | Wave 66 / TinkerCAD |
| BL-0624 | **Virtual oscilloscope component** — A draggable instrument component that can be placed on the schematic/breadboard canvas, connected via probe wires, and shows live waveforms during simulation. Adjustable time/div (horizontal) and auto-scaled voltage (vertical). More discoverable and tactile than the current separate graph panels. TinkerCAD and Falstad both have this. | DONE | C3 | Wave 69 |
| BL-0625 | **Virtual multimeter component** — Draggable multimeter instrument with two probe connections and a mode selector (DC voltage, AC voltage, current, resistance). Shows real-time readings during simulation. Far more intuitive than reading node voltages from a table — matches the physical bench experience. | DONE (Wave 72) | C3 | Wave 66 / TinkerCAD |
| BL-0626 | **Virtual function generator component** — Draggable signal source instrument: sine / square / triangle / sawtooth waveform selection, configurable frequency (Hz–MHz), amplitude, DC offset, and duty cycle (for square). Connects to circuit inputs. Used for AC analysis, filter characterization, and PWM testing without requiring a real function generator. | DONE (Wave 73) | C3 | Wave 66 / TinkerCAD |

### Cross-Tool Integration — All Domains (Wave 65 Audit)

> These are integration gaps between features that exist independently but don't talk to each other. Each one chips away at the "one tool" promise.

| ID | Description | Status | Complexity | Source |
|----|-------------|--------|------------|--------|
| BL-0559 | **PCB → Schematic back annotation** — BackAnnotationManager extended with PCB-specific methods (syncRefDesChange, syncPropertyChange). 29 tests. | DONE (Wave 139) | C4 | Wave 65 audit |
| BL-0560 | **Simulation results overlay on schematic canvas** — After running a DC operating point or transient simulation, node voltages and branch currents should optionally render as colored annotations directly on the schematic (probe labels at nodes, current arrows on wires). Currently simulation results exist only in the SimulationPanel — there is zero visual connection between simulation output and the schematic that produced it. | DONE (Wave 79) | C4 | Wave 65 audit |
| BL-0561 | **PDN/SI analysis reads actual routed PCB geometry** — pcb-geometry-bridge.ts with extractTraceGeometries() + traceGeometryToPdnInput/SiInput converters. 34 tests. | DONE (Wave 140) | C4 | Wave 65 audit |
| BL-0562 | **Thermal analysis reads actual component placement** — pcb-thermal-bridge.ts with PACKAGE_THERMAL_DB (15 packages) + extractThermalComponents(). 30 tests. | DONE (Wave 139) | C4 | Wave 65 audit |
| BL-0563 | **BOM back-annotation to schematic** — BackAnnotationManager singleton with findMatchingInstances(), generateBomBackAnnotationPatch(), patch CRUD. 38 tests. | DONE (Wave 139) | C4 | Wave 65 audit |
| BL-0564 | **Assembly cost estimator reads actual BOM** — `AssemblyCostEstimator` uses a manually entered part list. It should read from the current project's `bom_items` table as its input, so users don't re-enter data they've already captured in the BOM. | DONE (Wave 82) | C3 | Wave 65 audit |
| BL-0565 | **Inline supplier stock/price in BOM view** — The BOM table shows manufacturer and MPN but not real-time stock or pricing. Supplier data (from `supplier-apis.ts`, once real — see BL-0485) should be surfaced inline in the BOM row (stock badge, best price, lead time) without requiring a trip to the Procurement view. | DONE (verified Wave 106) | C4 | Wave 65 audit |
| BL-0566 | **DRC/PCB violation click → navigate to PCB canvas** — Clicking a PCB DRC violation in the ValidationView navigates to the Schematic (because the existing `validation-focus-pulse` logic was built for architecture/schematic). PCB DRC violations should switch the active view to PCB, center the viewport on the offending trace/pad, and pulse it. | DONE (Wave 78) | C3 | Wave 65 audit |
| BL-0567 | **Schematic component values → SPICE model auto-population** — When a resistor with `value: "10k"` is placed in the schematic, generating SPICE for simulation should auto-use that value rather than requiring the user to re-enter it in the SPICE editor. Currently the SPICE generator and the circuit instance schema are not linked — SPICE element values must be specified separately. | DONE | C3 | Wave 70 |
| BL-0568 | **Design snapshot restore cascade** — analyzeSnapshotDomains() + generateRestorePlan() with cross-domain dependency warnings. 46 tests. | DONE (Wave 140) | C4 | Wave 65 audit |
| BL-0569 | **Global cross-domain search** — There is no single search box that queries across schematic instances + BOM items + architecture nodes + community library + standard library + design history simultaneously. Finding "where is my LM7805?" requires checking five different panels manually. A unified search (like VS Code's Ctrl+P but for design objects) would dramatically improve navigation on complex projects. | DONE | C4 | Wave 70 |

### Cross-Tool Integration — Second Pass (Wave 65 Audit, continued)

| ID | Description | Status | Complexity | Source |
|----|-------------|--------|------------|--------|
| BL-0570 | **Architecture node → spawn schematic instance** — An architecture node typed as "Arduino Mega" or "LM7805" has zero connection to the schematic. Right-clicking a node should offer "Create schematic instance" which opens the schematic view and places the corresponding component. Without this, the architecture diagram is a whiteboard that contributes nothing to the rest of the design. | DONE | C4 | Wave 70 |
| BL-0571 | **Schematic ↔ Breadboard shared netlist** — The schematic canvas and breadboard view both represent the same physical circuit but share zero data. Placing a component in the schematic should offer to add it to the breadboard, and breadboard wire connections should be reflected as schematic nets. This is Fritzing's core differentiator — ProtoPulse has both views but no bridge between them. | DONE (verified Wave 106) | C5 | Wave 65 audit |
| BL-0572 | **DFM violations → highlight on PCB canvas** — `DfmChecker` results are displayed as a text list in the procurement/validation area. Clicking a violation should navigate to the PCB view and highlight the affected component/courtyard, exactly as BL-0566 does for DRC violations. Currently DFM and the PCB canvas are completely disconnected. | DONE (Wave 108) | C3 | Wave 65 audit |
| BL-0573 | **Design Variables ↔ SPICE simulation parameters** — `DesignVariablesPanel` stores named parameters (e.g. `Vcc = 5`, `R_load = 1k`). These should be importable as SPICE `.param` directives so the simulation automatically uses your design's variables instead of requiring manual re-entry. Currently the two systems exist independently with zero connection. | DONE (verified Wave 106) | C3 | Wave 65 audit |
| BL-0574 | **Monte Carlo ↔ BOM component tolerances** — bom-tolerance-bridge.ts with parseTolerance(), DEFAULT_TOLERANCE_BY_KEYWORD, bomItemsToToleranceSpecs(). tolerance column added to bom_items. 26 tests. | DONE (Wave 139) | C4 | Wave 65 audit |
| BL-0575 | **AI Chat → trigger and deliver exports** — The AI can discuss exports and explain how to use them, but it cannot actually invoke an export and provide a download link. Add AI tools `trigger_export` and `get_export_status` so the AI can say "I'll export this as KiCad 7 for you" and follow through. Currently the entire export system is unreachable from AI. | DONE | C3 | Wave 70 |
| BL-0576 | **AI Chat → simulation results in system prompt context** — Simulation output (DC operating voltages, transient waveform peaks, Monte Carlo yield, thermal hotspots) is never included in the AI's context. AI answers about "why is my circuit misbehaving" have zero access to what the simulation actually showed. Add a `buildSimulationContext()` function that summarizes the most recent simulation result and includes it in the system prompt. | DONE | C3 | Wave 70 |
| BL-0577 | **Digital Twin → out-of-spec telemetry creates validation issues** — When a connected device sends telemetry that exceeds a component's operating bounds (e.g. VCC = 4.1V, temperature = 87°C), the Digital Twin should automatically create a `validation_issue` record (type: "hardware-telemetry", severity: "warning") so it shows up in the ValidationView. Currently the Digital Twin and the validation system have zero connection. | DONE (Wave 132) | C4 | Wave 65 audit |
| BL-0578 | **Engineering Calculators → apply result to design** — After computing a result in any calculator (Ohm's Law: R = 330Ω; RC filter: C = 100nF), there should be an "Add to BOM" and "Apply to component" button that uses the result as the value for a selected BOM item or schematic instance. Currently results must be manually transcribed — the calculators are completely isolated from the rest of the design. | DONE (verified Wave 106) | C3 | Wave 65 audit |
| BL-0579 | **Export → auto-create design snapshot** — export-snapshot.ts with shouldAutoSnapshot + createExportSnapshot for manufacturing formats, 41 tests. | DONE (Wave 107) | C3 | Wave 65 audit |
| BL-0580 | **Validation ↔ BOM completeness warnings** — BOM items with missing MPN, blank manufacturer, or unverified specs should generate validation issues (severity: "info" or "warning") in the ValidationView, e.g. "R3 has no MPN — cannot verify for manufacturing." Currently BOM and validation are entirely decoupled; you can submit a BOM full of blank fields and the validator never notices. | DONE (Wave 85) | C4 | Wave 65 audit |
| BL-0581 | **Standards Compliance → unified ValidationView** — `StandardsCompliance` checker (IPC-2221, IPC-7711, RoHS, etc.) produces a separate panel/report. Its results should appear as first-class items in the `ValidationView` alongside DRC and ERC violations, with the same severity badges, group-by, filter, and click-to-navigate UX. Currently standards compliance is a completely separate surface. | DONE (Wave 133) | C4 | Wave 65 audit |
| BL-0582 | **Unified component search across Standard Library + Community Library + BOM** — Users must search Standard Library and Community Library in separate panels, and there is no way to search BOM items from either. A single "Find component" search (Ctrl+K equivalent) that queries all three simultaneously and surfaces the best match — with an "Add to BOM" or "Place on schematic" action — would eliminate most of the cross-panel navigation overhead. | DONE (Wave 129) | C4 | Wave 65 audit |
| BL-0583 | **Design Patterns → place schematic instances (not just arch nodes)** — SnippetCircuitInstance/SnippetCircuitNet types, prepareForPlacement() extended with ID remapping + position offsetting, 3 built-in snippets with circuit data. 60 tests. | DONE (Wave 139) | C4 | Wave 65 audit |
| BL-0584 | **Arduino Workbench → Knowledge Hub compile error linking** — When a compile error appears in the Workbench console, pattern-match it against known error signatures ("undefined reference to", "library not found", "no such file") and surface an inline link to the relevant Knowledge Hub article or a one-liner fix hint. Currently the 20-article knowledge hub is completely unreachable from the Workbench. | DONE (verified Wave 106) | C3 | Wave 65 audit |
| BL-0585 | **Component Editor → SPICE model / subcircuit attachment** — Custom components defined in the Component Editor have no SPICE model field. When those components are placed in a schematic and a simulation runs, they are silently ignored or cause missing-model errors. Add a SPICE subcircuit (`.subckt`) text field to component definitions so custom ICs and modules can participate in simulation. | DONE (verified Wave 106) | C3 | Wave 65 audit |
| BL-0586 | **Export panel → one-click complete fab package** — There is no single action that produces a complete manufacturing package (Gerbers + drill + BOM CSV + CPL CSV + readme.txt) as a single zip download. This bundle is only reachable by going through the full PCB ordering wizard. Add a standalone "Download fab package" button in the Export panel that generates it without requiring an order to be placed. | DONE (Wave 127) | C4 | Wave 65 audit |
| BL-0644 | **Export / ordering / simulation flows need explicit circuit selection** — Several multi-circuit project flows still default to `circuits[0]` when exporting, ordering, or running analysis. Require an explicit circuit target or stable project-level default before generating user-facing artifacts. | DONE (verified Wave 106) | C3 | Repo gap audit 2026-03-13 |
| BL-0650 | **Design Variables should be project-backed design data** — `DesignVariablesPanel` still persists variables in browser storage. Move them into project/export/history/collaboration flows so named parameters survive reloads, devices, and project handoff. | DONE (Wave 124) | C3 | Repo gap audit 2026-03-13 |
| BL-0651 | **Circuit Code preview must materialize into real schematic/project state** — The DSL pipeline currently stops at read-only preview. Add a safe "Apply to project" flow that creates real design objects, supports undo, and makes Circuit Code a true authoring path instead of a sandbox. | DONE (Wave 131) | C4 | Repo gap audit 2026-03-13 |
| BL-0652 | **Architecture expansion needs semantic pin mapping and better part matching** — The architecture→schematic expansion route still warns that it uses each part's first pin as a placeholder. Replace placeholder wiring with semantic connector mapping and higher-fidelity part resolution. | DONE (Wave 124) | C3 | Repo gap audit 2026-03-13 |
| BL-0655 | **Generative design candidates need apply/export/adopt workflows** — The generative design surface can evolve and score candidates, but winning options remain dead ends. Add actions to compare against the current design and promote a candidate into real project state. | DONE (verified Wave 106) | C3 | Repo gap audit 2026-03-13 |

## P3 — Low (Nice-to-Have / Long-Term Vision)


### Competitive Gaps — Core EDA (vs. KiCad/Altium/EasyEDA) (Omni-eval 2026-07-01)

| ID | Description | Status | Complexity | Source |
|----|-------------|--------|------------|--------|
| BL-1060 | **No IPC-2581/ODB++ export in the engine; legacy generators are unported debt magnets** — `packages/export/src` has neither format; the legacy generators (`server/export/ipc2581-generator.ts`, `server/export/odb-plus-plus-generator.ts`) exist but are confirmed complexity hotspots (`.ref/project-dna.md:90` — `getStandardLayers` CCN 75 in odb-plus-plus-generator.ts:98, `indent` CCN 38 in ipc2581-generator.ts). Legacy already validates these formats (BL-0532, DONE Wave 89) so the format knowledge exists — porting to the engine's byte-exact golden-file discipline would both close the gap and pay down the CCN-75 debt at once, rather than requiring a fresh from-scratch implementation. | OPEN | C4 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: competitive-core-eda) |
| BL-1061 | **No teardrops or zone via-stitching in the engine** — `packages/route/src` exports only `geom/obstacles/walkaround/shove/pour`; `rg` finds no teardrop/stitch code anywhere in `packages/`. KiCad 8 ships native teardrops (per-pad properties, bulk edit) and zone via-stitching. Legacy already shipped both as AI tools — BL-0102 (auto_stitch_vias, point-in-polygon grid) and BL-0103 (generate_teardrops) — both DONE but legacy-only, a different surface than the engine. Teardrops protect annular-ring breakout on cheap-fab tolerances and via stitching is standard on RF/ground-pour boards; both are pure geometry over the engine's existing pour/obstacle machinery. Cross-reference: BL-0103. | OPEN | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: competitive-core-eda) |
| BL-1062 | **No reusable design blocks/circuit snippets anywhere in ProtoPulse** — the `OpBody` union (`packages/graph/src/ops.ts:274-310`, confirmed exhaustive) has no clone/instantiate-fragment op; the legacy `design_patterns` view is rated Partial in `docs/FEATURE_MATURITY.md` ('no action to place/import a pattern or snippet into Schematic'). KiCad 9 shipped Design Blocks (reusable schematic-fragment libraries) plus multichannel PCB-layout replication as a headline feature. The engine's op-log makes a snippet uniquely strong here — a snippet is a replayable op sequence with ID remapping, so placement, wiring, and even routed layout could travel together, something KiCad 9 still can't do for PCB. | OPEN | C4 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: competitive-core-eda) |
| BL-1063 | **The `annotate` op exists but has zero schematic UI outside the Puzzle feature** — `packages/graph/src/ops.ts:264` ships `zAnnotate {anchor, text}` in the op union with full inverse/merge semantics, but `rg 'annotate'` in `packages/app/src` matches only `puzzle/puzzles.ts`, `puzzle/PuzzlePanel.tsx`, and `puzzle/puzzle.test.ts` — no toolbar text tool, no canvas rendering of annotations. Legacy shipped an equivalent freetext tool as BL-0492 (DONE, Wave 69, a different surface — legacy client) but that never touched the engine. Design notes ('DNP', 'match to R3') are part of every professional schematic; since the op is already coverage-gated, this is a canvas-render + toolbar-button distance from shipping. Cross-reference: BL-0492. | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: competitive-core-eda) |

### Competitive Gaps — Web/Maker Tools (vs. Wokwi/Flux/Falstad/Tinkercad) (Omni-eval 2026-07-01)

| ID | Description | Status | Complexity | Source |
|----|-------------|--------|------------|--------|
| BL-1064 | **No WiFi/network stack in the ESP32-S3 emulator** — ROADMAP.md:1330-1337 (slice 96) explicitly cuts 'WiFi/Bluetooth MAC, PHY, coexistence, or host-controller model'; only host-injected `RTC_WIFI_TRIG_EN`/`RTC_BT_TRIG_EN` wake bits exist (`packages/emu/src/esp32s3.ts:1673,4859`). `rg 'wifi|802.11|tcp|mqtt'` over `packages/emu/src` finds no MAC/IP/TCP surface, and no open backlog item covers it. WiFi/IoT firmware is the #1 reason people buy an ESP32-S3, so the from-scratch S3 emulator currently can't run the dominant real-world workload. A first slice need not be a full 802.11 PHY — a host-bridged virtual netif (an `esp_netif`-level trap analogous to the emulator's existing ROM-function host intercepts, bridging to fetch/WebSocket) would unlock HTTP/MQTT firmware far sooner. | OPEN | C5 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: competitive-web-maker) |
| BL-1065 | **No read-only embed mode for engine share links** — serverless `#d=` fragment share links exist (ROADMAP.md:2044-2048) but always load into the full editor after a confirm dialog; `rg -i 'embed|iframe|readonly'` over `packages/app/src` returns nothing. CircuitLab and Falstad both grow through iframe embeds across blog posts, forums, and course pages. Legacy already shipped BL-0377 ('Public embed API for schematic/PCB views,' DONE Wave 106, a different surface) but that work never touched the engine. Since the share link already carries the whole op-log (branches included) with zero server, a `?embed=1` readonly mode (pan/zoom + 'open in editor' + optional history-replay scrub) is a small delta on existing code — and could uniquely embed a replayable design history, something no competitor offers. Cross-reference: BL-0377. | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: competitive-web-maker) |
| BL-1066 | **No community part-pack registry, not even a static index** — `pp-part-pack` format ships with namespaces/provenance tiers/localStorage persistence (`ROADMAP.md` v0.6), but "registry/sharing" is an explicit open checkbox ("needs Tyler: where packs live, who can publish, moderation") and no BL item tracks it. A zero-infrastructure first slice — a git-repo-backed `index.json` of pack URLs + provenance tiers, fetched by the palette's existing pack loader, moderated via GitHub PRs — unblocks "browse and install community packs" without committing to hosting. | OPEN | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: competitive-web-maker) |
| BL-1067 | **@protopulse/emu ships `"private": true` — the only open TS ESP32-S3 core stays invisible** — `packages/emu/package.json` has `"private": true` and a description still claiming only "cycle-accurate AVR cores" (confirmed verbatim) despite the S3 core being feature-complete and gate-certified 2026-06-23 (crypto accelerators, GDMA, TWAI, LEDC/RMT, deep-sleep, all in TS). Wokwi's AVR/RP2040 cores (avr8js/rp2040js, both dependencies here) are open; its ESP32 family cores are proprietary — no open TS ESP32-S3 emulator exists on npm. Publishing the S3 core with a standalone demo page is a high-leverage credibility artifact and fixes the stale package description as a side effect. | OPEN | C2 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: competitive-web-maker) |

Nobody has this." ProtoPulse has the op-log graph a competitor can't easily copy, but hasn't shipped the CI packaging behind its own most-quoted differentiator. | OPEN | C4 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: moonshots) |
| BL-1075 | **Unified circuit+firmware time-travel debugger — op-log replay and co-sim execution are disconnected** — `packages/app/src/state/replay.ts:4-8` implements pure, graph-free op-log replay for the History panel; `packages/app/src/panels/CosimPanel.tsx:36-46` documents co-sim as a separately invoked bounded-window run, not indexed against op-log position. No code path re-materializes the graph at a scrub position AND re-runs emu/cosim state to the same point. Wiring `@protopulse/graph` replay to `@protopulse/emu`/`@protopulse/cosim` would let a user scrub circuit edits and firmware execution on one synchronized timeline. Note: legacy's BL-0703 (StateReplayEngine, DONE Wave 154) ships a conceptually similar time-travel replay but on the legacy client surface, not the op-log-native engine — this is a distinct, not-yet-migrated capability. Cross-reference: BL-0703. | OPEN | C5 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: moonshots) |

Add an emu/cosim-scoped tool slice (`load_firmware`, `run_cosim_window`, `assert_pin_trace`) so the crew can author firmware testbenches as typed, reviewable ops and store them as reusable regression fixtures — extending the crew's "reviewable, blameable, undoable" op discipline (`packages/ai/README.md`) to firmware test authorship. | OPEN | C5 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: moonshots) |
| BL-1077 | **GitHub-native part-pack registry — the one moonshot already blocked purely on a product decision** — `packages/parts/src/pack.ts:10-12` documents the pack format as deliberately hosting-free ("There is no registry or hosting here — that's a product decision"), and `ROADMAP.md:2081` still lists "Community library: registry/sharing (product decision — needs Tyler...)" as open. Given the pack format is already versioned, namespaced, and provenance-tiered (`pack.ts:43-68`), a GitHub-native registry (index repo + `protopulse pack search/install <id>` reading `raw.githubusercontent.com`, signing keyed to the `verified` provenance tier) needs zero new servers and resolves the "needs Tyler" ambiguity with a concrete, philosophy-consistent design. | OPEN | C4 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: moonshots) |

Combining these — one relay room per assignment, one branch per student, an instructor dashboard live-diffing every student branch against the baseline via `@protopulse/graph`'s `diff()` — gives real-time in-progress visibility legacy's static submit-and-grade classroom mode (BL-0305, DONE Wave 150, different surface) structurally cannot. Cross-reference: BL-0305. | OPEN | C5 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: moonshots) |

Legacy's BL-0377 (public embed API, DONE Wave 106) embeds a static `client/`-based view, not portable and not scrubbable. Per the no-bulk-scripts rule, hand-craft each one individually rather than templating. Cross-reference: BL-0903. | OPEN | C3 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: code-health-engine) |
| BL-1083 | **parts seed library is one 2,943-line file holding all 62 `definePart` calls — split by component class before the board/sensor breadth campaign (BL-0889→0901 pattern) multiplies it.** `packages/parts/src/seed/index.ts` is the designated growth point for every new co-sim device model going forward. Split now into `seed/passives.ts`, `seed/ics.ts`, `seed/connectors.ts`, `seed/boards.ts` (re-exported by `seed/index.ts`, zero API change) so per-file review stays tractable and the Hardware Verification Protocol's provenance comments (datasheet-verified pins, 'unverified — replace per-MPN before fab') are easier to police in category files than in one growing blob — same trajectory as esp32s3.ts, caught ~4x earlier. | OPEN | C1 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: code-health-engine) |
| BL-1084 | **project-dna.md's 'ruleTraceWidth CCN 72' engine hotspot is a lizard TypeScript-parser artifact — the real function is ~15 lines, CCN ~4.** `packages/drc/src/run.ts:207-221` is a single-loop function ending well before `ruleViaGeometry` (~:224); lizard merged every arrow-function rule body across the file's :207-493 span into one reported function. `.ref/project-dna.md:90` repeats this as a headline engine complexity hotspot. By contrast `validateGraph` CCN 60 (`graph/invariants.ts:27`) is a genuine single function and is correctly reported. Fix the project-dna.md entry, and note the lizard-TS merged-span failure mode in the `.ref` generation method so future `/initref` refreshes sanity-check top hotspots against actual function spans before publishing — a false CCN-72 in drc misdirects refactoring effort away from the real monolith (esp32s3.ts). | OPEN | C1 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: code-health-engine) |

### Security & Robustness (Omni-eval 2026-07-01)

| ID | Description | Status | Complexity | Source |
|----|-------------|--------|------------|--------|
| BL-1085 | **`PUBLIC_API_PATHS` prefix-matches `/api/settings/chat` without a trailing-slash boundary, unlike every sibling entry** — `server/request-routing.ts:5-14`'s `isPublicApiPath()` does `path.startsWith(publicPath)`; every other multi-route entry ends in `/` (`/api/auth/`, `/api/vault/`, `/api/parts/browse/`), correctly scoping the prefix match. `/api/settings/chat` (line 11) has no trailing slash, so any future route registered under that literal prefix (e.g. `/api/settings/chatbot-key`) silently becomes unauthenticated the instant it's added — the global auth gate at `server/index.ts:225-226` trusts this list wholesale. Currently harmless (only the exact GET/PATCH routes exist under the prefix per `server/routes/settings.ts:131-194`), but a one-line fix removes the footgun class entirely. | OPEN | C1 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: security-robustness) |

### Licensing & Legal Compliance (Omni-eval 2026-07-01)

| ID | Description | Status | Complexity | Source |
|----|-------------|--------|------------|--------|
| BL-1091 | **All 16 `@protopulse/*` engine packages have `license: null`, reported as UNLICENSED by external scanners** — verified: every `packages/*/package.json` (ai, app, cli, content, cosim, drc, emu, erc, export, graph, parts, relay, renderer, review, route, sim) is missing the `license` field, versus root `package.json`'s correct `"license": "MIT"`. All are `private: true` so no accidental npm publish risk, but it's a one-line-per-file fix (`"license": "MIT"`) that removes 16 false-positive flags from any future compliance scan and protects provenance if any package (`@protopulse/graph`, `@protopulse/emu`) is ever extracted for standalone open-source publishing. | OPEN | C1 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: critic:licensing-legal-compliance) |

### Data Format & Schema-Versioning (Omni-eval 2026-07-01)

| ID | Description | Status | Complexity | Source |
|----|-------------|--------|------------|--------|
| BL-1092 | **No ADR reserves how deliberate .ppx version migrations will actually be authored, despite the format's own README codifying 'never guessed at'** — `packages/graph/README.md:65-66`: "unknown shapes are rejected, never guessed at." `rg -n 'version|migrat' ROADMAP.md | grep -i 'ppx|format|schema'` and `find docs -iname '*ppx*'` both return zero hits — no ADR or ROADMAP entry addresses the migration mechanism. The only migration-labeled code in the repo, `packages/cli/src/legacy.ts`, is the one-time legacy-Postgres→.ppx importer, not a ppx-vN→vN+1 upgrader. This is a documentation-only companion to the PPX_FORMAT_VERSION gap: reserving the concern with an ADR now is cheap and prevents an ad hoc decision under pressure later. | OPEN | C1 | Omni-eval 2026-07-01 (lens: critic:ppx-schema-versioning-migration) |

| BL-0711 | Rename `.claude/hooks/never-idle-while-waiting.sh` to reflect new decision-ladder semantics (e.g. `wait-time-advisor.sh`). Hook was rewritten 2026-04-18 to replace "NEVER IDLE" pressure with a 3-tier ladder (BEST in-session work / OK wait-with-status / BAD past-cap dispatching). Filename still says "never-idle" which misrepresents the contract. Rename requires: `.claude/hooks/*.sh` move, update `.claude/settings.json` hook registration, grep all docs/memory for hook-name references. XS effort, clean single-wave cleanup. | OPEN | C1 | Session 2026-04-18 cleanup after 17-agent overdispatch incident |
| BL-0712 | Stop hook spam-loop fix — the Stop hook fires near-identical "agents in-flight, cannot verify" feedback on every stop attempt, creating a hold→stop-hook→"Holding"→stop-hook cycle with no new information between iterations. Tyler 2026-04-18 feedback: "Is there a way to update or improve the stop hooks to where they don't constantly run over and over and over and over and over again." Fixes: (1) state-hash deduplication — sidecar `~/.claude/hooks/.stop-hook-last-fire.json` tracks last-fire agent-ID set + timestamp; if same agents still in-flight AND < 2 min since last fire, suppress re-fire or emit one-liner; (2) progress detection — if `git log --since=lastfire` shows no new commits AND no agent completion notifications since last fire, skip the full verification nag; (3) max-fire cap — after 3 fires per stop sequence, emit "N warnings, allowing stop" and exit 0 without blocking; (4) exponential verbosity backoff — 1st fire full detail, 2nd summary, 3rd one-liner, 4th+ silent; (5) scope limit — don't block on agents that were spawned in this session and whose completion the assistant has already acknowledged. Goal: signal when something changes, not constant repetition. | OPEN | C2 | Session 2026-04-18 Tyler rage-feedback |

Debug with breakpoints, inspect registers and memory, fast-forward time. PlatformIO supports this via `test_speed = host`. Enables full development and testing workflow for users without hardware at hand. | DONE (verified Wave 106) | C5 | Wave 66 / PlatformIO |

## Completed Work Summary

51 waves of implementation have been completed. The following is a summary, not an exhaustive list:

| Domain | Key Completions |
|--------|----------------|
| **Security** | Wave A: ownership guards (22 files/100+ routes), DRC sandbox, URL validation, cache clear. Wave E: CORS allowlist, SPICE sanitization, tool confirmation, session resilience, graceful shutdown. |
| **Core EDA** | Push-and-shove routing, differential pair routing + meander, maze autorouter, trace router, footprint library (27 packages), copper pour, board stackup, PCB DRC (10 rules), net-class rules. |
| **Simulation** | DC operating point, AC analysis, transient simulation, Monte Carlo tolerance, thermal analysis, PDN analysis, signal integrity (transmission line + crosstalk + eye diagram), design variables. |
| **Hardware** | WebSerialManager (15 board profiles), SerialMonitorPanel, digital twin (telemetry protocol + device shadow + firmware templates), camera component ID, pinout hover (13 pinouts). |
| **AI** | 88 tools across 12 modules, agentic AI loop, generative design (fitness scoring + circuit mutation), confidence badges, AI review queue, architecture analyzer. |
| **Collaboration** | WebSocket rooms, CRDT ops, live cursors, role enforcement, offline sync, IndexedDB, service worker. |
| **Manufacturing** | PCB ordering (5 fab profiles), assembly cost estimator, LCSC/JLCPCB mapping, DFM checker (15 rules), ODB++ export, IPC-2581 export, Gerber, drill, pick-and-place generators. |
| **Import/Export** | 8 format parsers (KiCad, Eagle, Altium, gEDA, LTspice, Proteus, OrCAD, Generic), STEP 3D export, SPICE netlist parser, FZPZ handler, supplier APIs (7 distributors). |
| **Learning** | Tutorial system (5 tutorials), electronics knowledge hub (20 articles), design patterns library, standard component library (100 components), community library. |
| **UI/UX** | 26 ViewModes, command palette, keyboard shortcuts (19 defaults), i18n framework, Kanban board, barcode scanning, QR labels, DRC constraint overlay, circuit design as code (CodeMirror + DSL). |
| **Audit Fixes** | Frontend audit: 113/113 complete. Backend audit: 110/116 (4 open, 2 partial). |

**Test suite:** ~8784 tests across 198 files, 0 failures.
